Use the Japanese phrase cards as listen-and-repeat practice. Browser speech is only a practice aid; it is not an official audio recording.

How to practice
  1. Read the Japanese phrase.
  2. Listen to the browser pronunciation.
  3. Repeat it aloud.
  4. Repeat again without looking at the English/Urdu meaning.

📚 Study Materials

The three supplied learning sources are used together: English, Urdu and the original Japanese textbook.

Chapter 1

Chapter 1

Basics for Truck Drivers

1. Trucks Support People’s Daily Lives and Work

1.1 The Role of Trucks

You are probably now starting your studies to become a truck driver in Japan. So, how active and important are trucks in Japan?

Trucks transport many different things, such as:

Daily food products

Clothing and general goods

Industrial products and parts

Construction materials

Liquids and gases such as petroleum and gas

Special items such as huge bridges and plant equipment

Trucks also deliver parcels for home delivery services and goods for convenience stores.

Transport is also carried out by trains, ships, and airplanes. However, after goods arrive at stations, ports, or airports, trucks transport them to the final destination.

In this way, trucks carry more than 90% of domestic freight in Japan. They support people’s daily lives and work.

Truck drivers can truly be called essential workers. An essential worker means a person whose job is necessary to support social life.

1.2 What Would Happen If Truck Transport Stopped?

Truck transport is very important for people’s work and daily life.

If truck transport stopped all over Japan, products, parts, medicines, and other goods could not be delivered. As a result, shops, factories, hospitals, and many other places across Japan would not be able to continue their work.

In Japan, immediately after the Great East Japan Earthquake in 2011, many trucks could not run because of a fuel shortage. For some time, people could not buy products at convenience stores and other shops.

In Europe and Asia, trucks sometimes stop because of strikes.

For example, in Spain, tanker trucks stopped because of a strike. Gasoline could not be delivered to gas stations, causing a fuel shortage. As a result, citizens faced serious problems, such as being unable to go shopping or visit hospitals.

If trucks do not transport goods, similar problems may also occur in factories, markets, construction sites, and many other places.

Truck drivers should understand that truck transport is extremely important for economic activity and daily life.

Chapter 1

Basics for Truck Drivers

1.3 Driver’s License System and the Difference Between Business Trucks and Private Trucks

◆ Driver’s License System

To drive a truck, you need a driver’s license that matches the size of the truck.

The vehicles you can drive depend on the type of license.

◆ Difference Between Business Trucks and Private Trucks

In Japan, there are two types of trucks:

Business trucks

Private trucks

Business Trucks

A business truck is a truck used by a trucking company that has permission from the Minister of Land, Infrastructure, Transport and Tourism.

Only business trucks are allowed to carry customers’ goods for payment.

The license plate of a business truck has:

Green background

White letters

It is also called a green-number truck.

Private Trucks

A private truck is used by a company, shop, or individual to carry their own goods.

A private truck cannot carry customers’ goods for payment.

The license plate of a private truck has:

White background

Green letters

It is also called a white-number truck.

2. Social Impact of Truck Accidents

2.1 Truck Accidents That Have a Major Social Impact

To transport goods safely, securely, and reliably, safety must always be the top priority.

Traffic accidents do not only take or injure people’s lives. They also affect the management of the company.

Compared with other vehicles, trucks are large and heavy. Once a truck causes an accident, it can easily become a serious accident.

Such accidents affect not only the victim, but also the life of the victim’s family.

They also cause great losses to the trucking industry and society as a whole.

You must not forget that an accident caused by one truck driver can affect many other truck drivers and trucking companies.

A business truck driver is completely different from an ordinary driver. A business truck driver is a professional driver.

You should have:

Professional awareness that you drive a truck as your job

A strong belief that you must never cause an accident

2.2 Situation of Rear-End Collisions

About half of traffic accidents involving business trucks are rear-end collisions.

Many rear-end collisions are caused by:

Careless driving

Driving without enough attention

Looking away while driving

Drowsy driving

Driving while overworked

On ordinary roads, many rear-end collisions happen when a truck hits a vehicle in traffic at a low speed.

On expressways, many serious rear-end collisions happen when a truck hits the last vehicle stopped in a traffic jam at high speed.

2.3 Situation of Intersection Accidents

Among traffic accidents involving business trucks, fatal accidents with pedestrians and bicycles at intersections account for nearly 30% of the total.

Common accident patterns are:

When turning left at an intersection, accidents with bicycles are common.

When turning right at an intersection, accidents with pedestrians are common.

Truck drivers must be especially careful at intersections.

2.4 Prevention of Accidents Caused by Health Problems

An accident caused by health problems means an accident where the driver becomes unable to continue driving because of illness.

For truck drivers who drive for long hours, good health is very important.

When your physical condition is poor, your attention and concentration become weaker. This can lead to accidents.

Lifestyle-related diseases such as:

High blood pressure

Diabetes

Obesity

can cause serious problems if they are ignored.

During driving, they may lead to:

Stroke

Heart attack

Serious accidents

To prevent accidents caused by health problems, drivers should:

Always take regular health checkups

Get enough sleep every day

Do moderate exercise

Avoid drinking too much alcohol

Manage their physical condition carefully

2.5 Use of Safety Management Devices

Digital Tachograph and Drive Recorder

Various safety management devices are used to prevent traffic accidents.

Digital Tachograph

A digital tachograph records information about vehicle operation, such as:

Speed

Driving time

Operation time

Depending on the model, it may also record:

Sudden braking

Sudden starts

Fuel consumption

Other driving data

By carefully analyzing these records, it becomes easier to give guidance on:

Safe driving

Eco-driving

Drive Recorder

A drive recorder records video while the vehicle is moving.

When there is an impact such as sudden braking or a collision, it records video before and after the event.

Depending on the model, it may also continuously record video.

These devices help companies and drivers understand driving behavior and improve safety.

3. Attitude Required as a Truck Driver

3.1 Greetings Inside the Company

Human relationships begin with greetings.

To build good relationships inside the company, pay attention to the following points:

Greet people with a smile.

Greet people in a cheerful voice.

Look at the other person’s eyes when greeting.

Greet others first by yourself.

3.2 Greetings Outside the Company

Customers expect truck drivers to:

Handle goods carefully

Keep time

Wear proper clothes

Maintain a neat appearance

Use polite language

Show good manners and attitude

A truck driver is the face of the company.

If you cannot greet customers properly, the image of the company becomes bad.

Understand this well and greet customers with a smile and a cheerful voice.

3.3 Personal Appearance

Personal appearance means clothing and behavior that do not make other people feel uncomfortable.

A person’s appearance shows their personality and attitude toward work.

If your appearance is bad, customers may get a bad impression or feel worried.

This is not only a problem for the truck driver personally. It is also a big negative point for the company.

Therefore, always try to keep a clean and proper appearance.

Sometimes, even if your clothes are neat at the beginning, they may become untidy while driving.

During breaks, check the following points:

Are your clothes neat?

Is your name tag attached correctly?

Are your hands and nails clean?

Are your nails too long?

Personal Appearance Checklist

Check the following points:

☐ Is your hair too long or dirty? ☐ If you wear a cap, is it straight and not tilted upward or sideways? ☐ Are you avoiding wrapping a towel around your head or hanging it around your neck? ☐ Is your beard properly shaved? ☐ Are your clothes dirty or untidy? ☐ If you wear a name tag, is it attached correctly? ☐ Are your collar and sleeves clean? ☐ Are your hands and nails clean? Are your nails not too long? ☐ Are you not stepping on the heels of your shoes? ☐ Are your shoes not extremely dirty?

3.4 Workplace Attitude

◆ Have Cooperation

A company is a group of people. Therefore, it is important that everyone working there has a spirit of cooperation.

Driving work is done by the truck driver. However, the company’s overall work is done by all employees together.

Work must be carried out through teamwork.

Understand your role in the company, cooperate with others, and fulfill your responsibility.

By doing so, the company can develop and grow, and employees can become happier.

◆ Communicate with Others

Driving is usually work done alone outside the company.

Because of this, it is important to communicate with managers and other truck drivers during the limited time you are at the company, such as during roll call.

Good communication improves human relationships.

When relationships become better:

It becomes easier to consult others.

Stress becomes lighter.

Work efficiency improves.

3.5 Basic Use of Language

◆ Language Is the Basis of Human Relationships

The way you use language is the basis of human relationships.

If you can use the correct words according to the person you are speaking with, your relationships will become better.

It is also important to communicate clearly and correctly.

Be careful of the following:

Speaking too fast

Speaking in a small voice

Not clearly saying the end of your sentences

These can make it difficult for the other person to understand you correctly.

◆ Language Used Toward Customers

The language that gives customers a good impression is language that is bright, sincere, and polite.

Even if you make a small mistake in wording, customers will understand more easily if you speak sincerely.

Pay special attention to the following points:

Speak cheerfully.

Look at the other person’s face when speaking.

Speak clearly.

Do not use unclear or vague expressions.

3.6 Keeping the Vehicle Organized and Clean

◆ Importance of 4S

4S means the four Japanese workplace principles:

Practicing 4S helps prevent accidents by:

Preventing distracted driving

Keeping a clear view

Maintaining a safe driving environment

Sorting — Seiri

Sorting means arranging things that are messy and separating what is necessary from what is unnecessary.

Unnecessary items should be removed.

For example, if delivery slips, maps, helmets, cardboard boxes, or other items are placed near the safety window on the passenger-side door, you may fail to notice pedestrians or others.

To prevent this, remove unnecessary items and keep the area clear.

Setting in Order — Seiton

Setting in order means placing necessary items properly in places where they are easy to use.

When organizing items, think carefully about where and how to place them so they do not disturb driving.

For example, if documents are placed on the dashboard, they may reflect on the windshield while driving and make visibility poor.

Also, if you become distracted by documents or other items, it may lead to careless or inattentive driving.

Be careful.

Cleaning — Seisou

Cleaning means keeping the inside of the vehicle clean and easy to use.

For example, if an empty can is lying on the floor and gets stuck under the brake pedal, you may not be able to press the brake.

This can lead to a serious accident.

Also, if the inside of the vehicle is messy when you start work, cleaning it will take extra time and waste time.

Make it a habit to clean the inside of the vehicle after finishing driving work.

Keeping Clean — Seiketsu

Keeping clean means maintaining the condition created by sorting, setting in order, and cleaning.

If sorting, setting in order, and cleaning are not done, you may lose the feeling of taking good care of the vehicle.

This can also lead to rough driving.

When sorting, setting in order, and cleaning are properly done, your awareness as a professional driver becomes stronger.

It also strengthens your awareness that the truck is a moving advertisement for the company.

A truck with the company name written on it advertises the company while it is running.

This awareness leads to safe driving with good manners.

3.7 To All Drivers Who Drive Trucks

◆ Learning Driving Skills According to Truck Characteristics

Most of a truck driver’s job is driving.

Therefore, everyone who drives a truck must properly learn driving skills.

Trucks have different characteristics from other vehicles. Drivers must understand these characteristics well and learn driving skills that match the special features of trucks.

◆ There Is a Trap When You Become Used to Driving

When you are not used to driving, you drive with tension and careful attention.

However, when you become used to driving, that tension becomes weaker, and it becomes easier to become careless.

Because of this, drivers may stop doing basic safety actions or stop doing things that they should do.

This can lead to accidents.

Do not forget the careful attitude and sense of tension you had when you first started driving.

4. Basics of Safe Driving

4.1 Daily and Holiday Health Management

◆ Importance of Health Management

For truck drivers who drive every day, it is important to always keep both the mind and body in good condition.

Driving has the following characteristics:

You must do it alone.

You must do it for long hours.

Sometimes, you must make quick decisions in a very short time.

In emergencies, you must make calm and correct decisions.

While driving, you continue sitting in the driver’s seat.

You move very little and cannot easily change your posture for a long time.

If you drive when your physical condition is bad, you cannot perform these tasks properly.

This may lead to an accident.

Keeping good health is an essential condition for safe driving.

◆ Points to Be Careful About in Daily Life and on Holidays

Truck drivers often have irregular lifestyles because of:

Late-night departures

Early-morning departures

Trips that require staying overnight

Therefore, in daily life, pay special attention to the following points:

Get enough sleep.

Eat well-balanced meals.

Have a place where you can relax.

Do exercise that does not leave you tired the next day.

Have a hobby that helps you refresh your mind.

Keep enough mental space and do not feel rushed.

On holidays, take proper rest.

Refresh both your mind and body through light exercise, hobbies, or other relaxing activities.

4.2 Eliminating Drunk Driving

◆ Drunk Driving Is a Crime

Drunk driving is a serious, dangerous, and malicious criminal act that can lead to major accidents.

For this reason, the penalties are severe.

Even if you do not cause an accident, you may receive punishments such as:

Imprisonment

A fine

Cancellation of your driver’s license

Suspension of your driver’s license

If you cause a personal injury accident while drunk driving, meaning an accident that kills or injures a person, you may be sent to prison.

No matter what the situation is, you must never drive after drinking alcohol.

◆ Even the Next Day, You Can Be Punished If Alcohol Still Affects You

Even on the day after drinking alcohol, if the effect of alcohol remains in your body, this is called a hangover.

If you drive in that condition, it is treated as drunk driving and you can be punished.

You cannot say:

“I slept after drinking, so it is okay.”

“Several hours have passed, so it is not drunk driving.”

If even a small effect of alcohol remains, you must not drive.

4.3 Obeying the Maximum Speed Limit

◆ On Expressways, the Maximum Speed Differs by Type of Truck

There are two types of maximum speed:

Legal speed limit

Regulated speed limit

Legal Speed Limit

The legal speed limit means the maximum speed decided by law when there are no road signs or road markings showing a specific speed limit.

On ordinary roads, the maximum speed for trucks is 60 km/h.

On national expressways, the maximum speed differs depending on the type of truck.

It is divided into three categories:

100 km/h

90 km/h

80 km/h

For medium-sized trucks, the maximum speed differs depending on:

Gross vehicle weight This means the weight of the vehicle when it is loaded with the maximum amount of cargo.

Maximum loading capacity This means the maximum weight of cargo that can be loaded onto the vehicle.

Drivers must be careful and know the correct speed limit for their truck.

◆ Regulated Speed Limit

A regulated speed limit means the maximum speed shown by road signs or road markings.

On ordinary roads, many places are regulated to:

40 km/h

30 km/h

On national expressways, speed may also be restricted during:

Rain

Snow

Strong wind

Other dangerous weather conditions

Pay close attention to speed limit signs and road markings while driving.

◆ The Maximum Speed on Motor-Vehicle-Only Roads Is the Same as Ordinary Roads

National expressways and motor-vehicle-only roads are together called expressways.

However, national expressways and motor-vehicle-only roads have different maximum speed limits.

The maximum speed on motor-vehicle-only roads is the same as ordinary roads.

If no speed is shown by road signs or road markings, the maximum speed is 60 km/h.

Be careful.

If you drive with the same feeling as on a national expressway, you may violate the speed limit.

4.4 Passing Through Intersections

◆ Checking Safety and Giving Signals

When you are going to change lanes, make a U-turn, or reverse, you must first check safety with mirrors and other methods.

After checking safety, give a signal.

When turning left or right, you must give the signal 30 meters before the place where you will turn, such as before the intersection.

◆ Slow Down When Turning Right or Left at Intersections

Vehicles must drive on the left side of the center of the road. If there is a center line, vehicles must drive on the left side of that center line.

At intersections, you must carefully watch:

Other vehicles

Pedestrians crossing the road

Bicycles crossing the road

You must drive at a safe speed and in a safe way.

Accidents are especially likely to happen when turning right or left.

Therefore, when turning right or left, you are required to drive slowly.

Driving slowly means driving at a speed where you can stop immediately.

Slow down enough so that you can stop safely before the crosswalk.

◆ Vehicles Going Straight or Turning Left Have Priority Over Vehicles Turning Right

At an intersection, vehicles going straight and vehicles turning left have priority over vehicles turning right.

Even if you enter the intersection first, you must not block vehicles going straight or turning left.

Wait until those vehicles pass.

After confirming safety, turn right.

◆ Passing Through Intersections Without Traffic Lights

At intersections without traffic lights, be especially careful about the following points:

If the crossing road is a priority road, slow down and do not disturb vehicles on that road.

If the crossing road is clearly wider, slow down and do not disturb vehicles on that road.

At intersections where the roads are about the same width, vehicles coming from the left have priority.

If a vehicle is approaching from the left, do not disturb its movement.

Important note: At intersections where the roads seem to be about the same width, your own road may look wider than the crossing road. Be careful not to make a wrong judgment.

4.5 Keeping a Safe Following Distance

◆ Keep Enough Distance So You Do Not Hit the Vehicle in Front Even If It Stops Suddenly

A common feature of truck accidents is that many of them are rear-end collisions.

To prevent rear-end collisions, it is important to keep enough following distance from the vehicle in front.

If you keep enough distance, you can respond safely even if the vehicle in front suddenly slows down or stops.

◆ Guide for Safe Following Distance

Use the following guide:

Examples

If you are driving on an ordinary road at 50 km/h:

50 - 15 = 35

So, keep at least 35 meters of distance.

If you are driving on an expressway at 100 km/h:

Keep at least 100 meters of distance.

◆ Keep Following Distance Consciously

In trucks, the driver’s seat is high, so the road surface is easy to see.

Because of this, truck drivers often feel that the distance from the vehicle in front is longer than it really is.

As a result, the following distance may become too short.

Always consciously keep enough following distance.

4.6 Prohibition of Using Smartphones While Driving

◆ Danger of Using Smartphones and Mobile Phones While Driving

If you operate a smartphone or mobile phone while driving, or stare at the screen, you may notice the stopped vehicle in front too late.

This can lead to accidents such as rear-end collisions.

For this reason, operating smartphones or looking closely at screens while driving is prohibited.

If you violate this rule, you will receive heavy punishment.

If you cause a traffic accident, you may receive imprisonment or a fine.

◆ Use Smartphones Only After Stopping in a Safe Place

If you must use a smartphone or mobile phone while driving, always stop the vehicle in a safe place, such as a parking area, before using it.

Do not stop at the left edge of the road to use your phone.

This is dangerous because:

A following vehicle may hit you from behind.

A large truck may block other traffic.

It may cause traffic congestion.

Also, even if only the ringtone sounds while driving, you may become distracted and lose attention to the road ahead.

While driving, turn off the phone, or set it to silent mode or drive mode.

4.7 Protection of Pedestrians and Others

◆ Keep a Safe Space

Even slight contact with a pedestrian or bicycle can lead to a serious personal injury accident.

When passing beside pedestrians or bicycles, keep a safe space.

If you cannot keep enough space, drive slowly.

A safe space means at least 1 meter or more, so that safety can be maintained even if a pedestrian or bicycle suddenly becomes unsteady.

◆ Points to Remember When Approaching a Crosswalk

When approaching a crosswalk without traffic lights, follow these rules:

If it is clear that there are no pedestrians or bicycles crossing, you may continue driving.

If it is not clear whether pedestrians or bicycles are crossing, slow down to a speed where you can stop before the crosswalk. If there is a stop line, slow down so you can stop before the stop line.

If pedestrians or bicycles are crossing or trying to cross, stop before the crosswalk and do not disturb their passage.

If a vehicle is stopped just before the crosswalk, stop before passing in front of the stopped vehicle and check the safety of the crosswalk.

The same rule applies when approaching a bicycle crossing lane.

4.7 Protection of Pedestrians and Others

Continued

◆ Protection of Small Children, Elderly People, and Others

When the following people are passing, you must either stop temporarily or drive slowly so that you do not disturb their movement:

Elderly pedestrians

Children or small children without a guardian

People using wheelchairs for persons with physical disabilities

Pedestrians with physical disabilities

People who have difficulty walking

Always drive with extra care around people who may need more time or space to move safely.

◆ Be Careful Near School Buses and Kindergarten Buses

When a school bus, kindergarten bus, or nursery school bus is stopped to let children get on or off, you must slow down and check safety carefully.

Children may suddenly run out from in front of or behind the bus.

Do not pass carelessly.

Drive slowly and be ready to stop at any time.

◆ Protection of People with Visual or Hearing Disabilities

When you see a person walking with a white cane, a guide dog, or other assistance, you must drive carefully.

Also, people with hearing disabilities may not notice vehicle sounds.

Do not assume that the pedestrian has noticed your truck.

Slow down or stop when necessary, and make sure you do not disturb their movement.

4.8 Priority for Emergency Vehicles

Emergency vehicles include vehicles such as:

Police cars

Fire engines

Ambulances

When an emergency vehicle approaches with its siren sounding and red warning light flashing, you must give way.

When an Emergency Vehicle Approaches Near an Intersection

If an emergency vehicle approaches near an intersection, move to the left side of the road and stop.

Do not block the emergency vehicle.

Give enough space so it can pass safely.

When an Emergency Vehicle Approaches on a Road Other Than an Intersection

On roads other than intersections, move to the left side of the road and give way.

In this case, stopping temporarily is not always required.

However, depending on the situation, stop temporarily so that you do not disturb the emergency vehicle.

On a One-Way Road

Even on a one-way road, the basic rule is to move to the left side and give way.

However, if moving to the left side would block the emergency vehicle, move to the right side and give way.

Emergency vehicles are allowed to drive partly on the right side of the road when necessary.

In that situation, vehicles coming from the opposite lane must also move to the left side and give way.

4.9 Passing Through Railway Crossings

If a large truck collides with a train at a railway crossing, it can cause a serious accident with many deaths and injuries.

Therefore, truck drivers must be especially careful when passing through railway crossings.

◆ When You Must Not Enter a Railway Crossing

You must not enter a railway crossing in the following situations:

1. When the Alarm Starts or the Barrier Starts Coming Down

Do not enter the crossing when:

The alarm begins to sound

The crossing barrier begins to come down

The barrier is already down

2. When the Road Ahead Is Congested

If the road beyond the crossing is congested, your truck may become stuck inside the crossing.

Before entering the crossing, always check the road condition ahead.

If there is not enough space for your truck beyond the crossing, never enter the crossing.

◆ How to Pass Through a Railway Crossing

To prevent the engine from stopping inside the crossing, do not change gears while crossing.

Pass through the crossing in the same low gear used when starting.

While crossing, pay attention to:

Pedestrians

Bicycles

Oncoming vehicles

Drive slightly toward the center so that the wheels do not fall off the edge of the crossing.

◆ What to Do If Your Vehicle Cannot Move Inside a Railway Crossing

If your vehicle cannot move inside a railway crossing, take action immediately.

1. Use the Emergency Button

At railway crossings with an alarm, use the emergency button attached to the alarm post or nearby equipment.

This button informs the railway side that there is an emergency.

2. Use a Flare

If there is no emergency button, use the flare installed in the vehicle.

Give a signal to the train as quickly as possible.

You must learn the correct way to use a flare.

Also, check in advance where the flare is kept inside the vehicle.

3. Use Smoke or Fire If There Is No Flare

If there is no flare, or if the flare has already been used up, burn something that produces smoke and give a warning signal to the train.

The important point is to inform the train as quickly as possible.

4.10 Driving on Expressways

◆ Vehicle Inspection

When driving on an expressway, the vehicle must not stop because of a shortage of fuel, coolant, or engine oil.

Before using an expressway, especially check the following points:

Is there enough fuel?

Is the coolant level within the required range?

Is the radiator cap securely closed?

Is the engine oil level suitable?

Is the fan belt tension suitable?

Is there any damage to the fan belt?

Is the tire air pressure suitable?

Are the tire grooves deep enough?

◆ Cargo Inspection

If cargo falls from a truck while driving at high speed, it is extremely dangerous.

Before driving on an expressway, you must check the cargo carefully so that it does not fall or fly off.

Take necessary action, such as reloading or securing the cargo again.

◆ Obeying Lane Rules

When driving on an expressway, follow the lane rules carefully.

Keep the rightmost lane, the overtaking lane, open for overtaking.

Drive slightly toward the left side of the driving lane.

Trailers must drive in the leftmost lane on national expressways, except when road signs or markings specify another lane.

On motor-vehicle-only roads, trailers must drive in the leftmost lane in sections where signs or markings specify this rule.

Do not drive on the road shoulder or roadside strip.

4.11 Driving on Slopes, Curves, and in Tunnels

◆ On Slopes, Uphill Vehicles Have Priority

On slopes, starting a vehicle on an uphill road is difficult.

Therefore, on a narrow road, a vehicle going downhill should give way to a vehicle going uphill.

However, if there is a nearby passing space where vehicles can temporarily move aside to pass each other, even the uphill vehicle should enter that space and wait.

◆ Be Careful of Brake Fade on Downhill Roads

On a long downhill road, if you use the foot brake too much, the brake lining can overheat.

When this happens, friction becomes weaker and the brakes may stop working properly.

This is called brake fade.

If brake fade happens on a downhill road, you cannot control your speed. This is extremely dangerous.

When driving down a long slope:

Use a low gear.

Use engine braking.

Use exhaust braking.

Reduce the use of the foot brake.

If the gear slips out unexpectedly, immediately use the foot brake to slow down or stop the truck sufficiently.

After that, put the gear in again.

◆ Slow Down Enough Before Curves

If you drive too fast on a curve, there is a danger that the vehicle may:

Slip

Fail to complete the turn

Go off the road

Roll over

Especially when you apply the brakes while turning the steering wheel, the truck becomes more likely to roll over.

Therefore, slow down enough before entering the curve.

S-shaped curves, where you must turn the steering wheel from one side to the other, are also easy places for rollover accidents.

Drive carefully and operate the steering wheel calmly.

◆ Accidents in Tunnels Can Become Serious Accidents

If an accident happens inside an expressway tunnel, many vehicles may become involved.

Therefore, drive carefully and follow these points:

When entering a tunnel at high speed, your vision may suddenly become worse.

The vehicle in front may slow down before the tunnel.

Reduce speed before entering the tunnel.

Keep enough following distance.

There may be traffic congestion inside the tunnel.

Before entering a tunnel, check traffic conditions using information boards or car radio.

When entering the tunnel, pay close attention to the situation ahead.

In tunnels with traffic lights, always obey the signals.

Even in tunnels with lighting, turn on your headlights.

Weather may be different at the entrance and exit of a tunnel.

When approaching the tunnel exit, pay attention to weather and road conditions.

4.12 Obeying Traffic Signals

◆ Yellow Signal Means Stop in Principle

A yellow signal means that you must stop before the intersection.

However, there are exceptions, such as when you are already close to the stopping position and stopping suddenly may cause the vehicle behind you to hit you.

Do not think, “The vehicle in front will enter the intersection, so I will also pass.”

If the vehicle in front stops, you may hit it from behind.

When the signal changes to yellow, slow down early and stop before the intersection.

◆ Flashing Red Signal Means Temporary Stop

At a flashing red signal:

Pedestrians may proceed while paying attention to other traffic.

Vehicles must stop temporarily at the stopping position.

After stopping, vehicles may proceed only after confirming safety.

At a flashing yellow signal:

Pedestrians and vehicles may proceed while paying attention to other traffic.

◆ Right-Turn Arrow Signal

Even if the traffic light facing you is red, if a blue right-turn arrow is shown below it, you may turn right or make a U-turn.

When turning right with a right-turn arrow signal, check carefully whether any oncoming vehicles are entering the intersection.

Important note:

If there is a sign or road marking that prohibits U-turns, you cannot make a U-turn at that intersection.

Always pay attention to signs and road markings.

4.13 Obeying Road Signs and Road Markings

◆ Road Signs and Road Markings

Road signs are signboards that show traffic regulations and other important information.

There are many signs that are especially important for trucks.

Road markings are lines, symbols, and letters painted on the road surface. They may also be shown by road studs or other markings.

Road markings include:

Regulatory markings

Instruction markings

◆ Road Signs and Road Markings Are Important Sources of Information

On ordinary roads, the legal maximum speed for vehicles is 60 km/h.

However, many roads are limited to:

40 km/h

30 km/h

These limits are shown by road signs or road markings.

Drivers must always look carefully at road signs and road markings and obey them.

◆ Important Signs for Truck Drivers

Truck drivers must pay special attention to signs such as:

Weight Limit

Vehicles whose gross vehicle weight is more than the weight shown on the sign cannot pass.

Height Limit

Vehicles whose height from the ground is more than the height shown on the sign cannot pass.

Lane Classification for Specific Types of Vehicles

This sign shows the traffic lane for large freight vehicles and similar vehicles when there are three or more lanes on one side.

No Entry Without Tire Chains

This sign means vehicles without tire chains cannot pass.

Even if the vehicle has winter tires, tire chains are still required when this sign applies.

Stop Sign

At an intersection with this sign, you must always stop temporarily.

Even if you think it is safe, you must stop first and then check safety.

4.14 Parking and Stopping

◆ Difference Between “Parking” and “Stopping”

There are two words used when a vehicle is stopped:

Parking

Stopping

These have different meanings.

In general, parking means the vehicle is stopped for a longer time or the driver is away from the vehicle.

Stopping means the vehicle is stopped for a short time, such as for people getting in or out, or for loading and unloading goods.

Truck drivers must understand the difference and follow the rules.

◆ Places Where Parking and Stopping Are Prohibited

Do not park or stop in places where parking or stopping is prohibited by signs or road markings.

Also, be careful around places such as:

Intersections

Crosswalks

Bicycle crossings

Railway crossings

Bus stops

Curves

Slopes

Tunnels

Areas where stopping will disturb traffic

Stopping a large truck in the wrong place can block other vehicles and create danger.

◆ Places Where Parking Is Prohibited

Parking is prohibited in the following places:

Places where parking is prohibited by road signs or road markings

Within 1 meter of a fire alarm

Within 3 meters of the entrance or exit of a parking lot, garage, or other vehicle entrance

Within 5 meters from the edge of a road construction area

Within 5 meters of a place where firefighting equipment or fire-prevention water tanks are placed

Within 5 meters of a fire hydrant, designated fire-water sign, or fire-prevention water tank intake

◆ Parking and Stopping on Expressways

On expressways, parking and stopping are prohibited except in special cases such as:

To prevent danger

Vehicle breakdown

Other unavoidable situations

Do not stop on an expressway without a valid reason.

◆ Rules for Parking and Stopping

Parking and stopping rules depend on:

Whether there is a sidewalk

Whether there is a roadside strip

The type and width of the roadside strip

Follow these points:

When stopping to let people get in or out, or to load and unload goods, stop as close as possible to the left side of the road.

Do not disturb other traffic.

On roads without sidewalks or roadside strips, park along the left side of the road.

Be careful not to park in a way that prevents other vehicles from passing.

On roads with sidewalks, park along the left side of the roadway.

If the roadside strip is 0.75 meters or less, you cannot enter the roadside strip. Park along the left edge of the roadway.

If the roadside strip is more than 0.75 meters, you may park inside it, but you must leave at least 0.75 meters of space on the left side for pedestrians.

◆ Roadside Strips Where Parking and Stopping Are Not Allowed

Even if the roadside strip is more than 0.75 meters wide, you cannot enter it when it has:

Two solid white lines This is a roadside strip for pedestrians.

One solid white line and one broken white line This is a roadside strip where parking and stopping are prohibited.

4.15 Driving at Night

When driving at night, it becomes more difficult to see outside the vehicle.

Therefore, do not turn on the interior light while driving at night.

The interior light can make it harder to see outside and can reduce your visibility.

Drive carefully at night and pay extra attention to:

Pedestrians

Bicycles

Road signs

Road markings

Vehicles ahead

Vehicles coming from the opposite direction

5. Basics of Driving Manners

5.1 Drive Safely and Politely as an Example for Other Vehicles

Drivers who drive business trucks must try to drive safely and politely, becoming a good example for other vehicles.

For drivers of ordinary vehicles, simply having a large truck driving nearby can feel frightening.

Therefore, truck drivers must never do dangerous driving such as:

Forcing another vehicle to the side This means driving too close to a vehicle traveling beside you.

Aggressive driving / tailgating This means dangerous driving that blocks or disturbs another vehicle’s movement.

5.2 Trucks Are “Moving Advertisements”

The names of companies and customers, trademarks, and product names are often written clearly on trucks.

Because of this, a truck carries three important images while driving:

The image of the company

The image of the customer

The image of the trucking industry

A truck is truly a moving advertisement.

Many people see trucks on the road, so trucks also have an advertising effect.

This also means that truck drivers have a duty and responsibility to care about other vehicles and pedestrians, and not make them feel worried or unsafe.

Truck drivers must always be an example for other drivers.

Have pride as a professional driver, learn good driving manners, and put them into practice.

5.3 The Basics of Driving Manners Are “Consideration” and “Giving Way”

Roads are public places used by many people.

Traffic rules are the minimum rules needed to use public roads safely and smoothly.

However, there are some situations that cannot be solved by traffic rules alone.

What solves these situations is driving manners, especially:

Consideration for others

Giving way to others

Truck drivers use the road as their workplace.

Therefore, they should always have the feeling that they are being allowed to use the road, and they should drive with the attitude of giving way to others.

5.4 Drive with Consideration for Others

Truck drivers should always think about other road users.

This includes:

Other vehicles

Pedestrians

Bicycles

Motorcycles

People living near the road

Do not drive in a way that makes others feel fear or danger.

Safe driving is not only about following rules. It is also about thinking from the other person’s point of view.

5.5 Give Way When Passing on Narrow Roads

Trucks are wide vehicles.

On narrow roads, if an oncoming vehicle is approaching and you continue driving forcefully, there is a risk of contact with the oncoming vehicle.

In such a situation, do not think:

“The other driver will probably stop.”

Instead, you should stop first and give way to the other vehicle.

When stopping close to the left side, be very careful not to hit:

Pedestrians

Bicycles

Roadside signs

Roadside structures

Objects near the edge of the road

5.6 Do Not Throw Things Out of the Vehicle

Some truck drivers throw things such as paper waste, plastic bottles, or empty cans out of the truck window.

This kind of littering is very bad manners.

It is also prohibited by law.

Littering while parked or stopped is also not allowed.

You must never throw things away outside the vehicle, whether you are:

Driving

Parked

Stopped

6. Practicing Eco-Driving

◆ Eight Points of Eco-Driving

The main points of eco-driving are the following eight items:

Start and accelerate gently.

Shift up early.

Drive at a steady speed.

Use engine braking.

Obey economical speed.

Use predictive driving to reduce stopping and starting.

Avoid unnecessary engine revving.

Keep idling to the minimum necessary.

1. Start and Accelerate Gently

The basic idea of eco-driving is not to raise the engine speed more than necessary.

Trucks are diesel vehicles.

Even when carrying heavy cargo, they can start and accelerate smoothly at low engine speeds.

Use the low engine-speed range as much as possible.

Start and accelerate slowly and gently.

2. Shift Up Early

If you increase speed while staying in a low gear, the engine speed becomes high and fuel efficiency becomes worse.

For example:

If a large vehicle drives in 4th gear instead of 5th gear

If a medium or small vehicle drives in 3rd gear instead of 4th gear

fuel efficiency becomes about 20% to 40% worse.

Therefore, shift up early and drive using a higher gear as much as possible.

3. Drive at a Steady Speed

Driving in waves, where you repeatedly press the accelerator and brake, makes fuel efficiency worse.

For example, compared with steady-speed driving, wave-like driving with speed changes of about 10 km/h can create a fuel-efficiency difference of about 10%.

Try to keep your speed as steady as possible.

To do this:

Look far ahead.

Avoid repeating acceleration and deceleration.

Keep a safe following distance.

4. Use Engine Braking

Diesel vehicles do not consume fuel when you release the accelerator while driving and the vehicle is in an engine-braking state.

In that condition, the vehicle moves forward by inertia.

Using engine braking is connected to eco-driving.

Before downhill roads and traffic lights:

Use engine braking early.

Use the foot brake as little as possible.

Do not drive with the clutch pressed down or the gear in neutral.

If you do this:

The vehicle becomes like it is idling.

Engine braking will not work.

Wait until just before stopping before putting the gear in neutral or pressing the clutch.

5. Obey Economical Speed

Economical speed means a speed at which you can drive with the lowest possible engine speed without disturbing the smooth flow of traffic.

For example, if 50 km/h is the economical speed according to surrounding traffic conditions, increasing the speed to 60 km/h will increase fuel consumption by about 10%.

Do not overtake unnecessarily.

Do not drive too fast.

6. Reduce Stopping and Starting Through Predictive Driving

Once a vehicle stops, it uses a large amount of fuel when starting again.

This fuel use is almost the same as driving uphill.

Therefore, try to predict traffic conditions ahead and avoid unnecessary stopping and starting.

For example:

Look far ahead.

Predict changes in traffic lights.

Keep enough distance from the vehicle in front.

Avoid sudden braking.

Avoid unnecessary acceleration.

This helps save fuel and also improves safety.

7. Avoid Unnecessary Engine Revving

Unnecessary engine revving means pressing the accelerator when it is not needed, especially when the gear is in neutral.

This wastes fuel and creates noise.

Some drivers do this as a habit without noticing.

When the gear is in neutral, be conscious and do not rev the engine unnecessarily.

8. Keep Idling to the Minimum Necessary

Unnecessary idling wastes fuel.

It can also disturb other people because of noise.

Stop the engine when you will be stopped for some time, such as during:

Loading and unloading

Waiting for cargo

Taking a break

Waiting for a long time at a railway crossing

When leaving the vehicle, do not forget to stop the engine.

One method is to use a key-removal rope, where the vehicle key is tied to a cord on the driver’s waist. This helps prevent the driver from forgetting to remove the key.

7. Proper Operation Route

7.1 Choosing a Route with Safety in Mind

Deciding the truck operation route is the job of the operation manager.

However, drivers must also understand the route and drive safely.

When choosing or following a route, safety must be considered first.

Do not only think about the shortest route or the fastest route.

A safe route should consider:

Road width

Traffic volume

Intersections

School zones

Residential areas

Height limits

Weight limits

Roads where trucks are prohibited

Places where stopping or loading/unloading may be difficult

A route that looks convenient may still be dangerous for a truck.

Always follow the company’s instructions and choose a route that allows safe operation.

7.2 Understanding Route Information in Advance

Before starting operation, understand the route information in advance.

Check important points such as:

Destination

Delivery place

Loading and unloading place

Road conditions

Traffic restrictions

Construction areas

Narrow roads

Low bridges or height restrictions

Weight restrictions

Places where turning may be difficult

Parking or stopping points

Rest areas

If you do not understand the route clearly, ask the operation manager or company staff before departure.

Do not make your own careless judgment while driving.

Preparing route information in advance helps prevent:

Accidents

Delays

Getting lost

Entering roads where trucks cannot pass

Dangerous reversing or turning

This completes Chapter 1: Basics for Truck Drivers.

Chapter 2

Chapter 2

Operation Duties

1. Work Flow and Rules for Truck Drivers

This section explains the general work flow of a truck driver.

If each company has its own rules, follow the company’s rules.

1. Coming to Work

Come to work with enough time so that you are not late.

When you arrive, greet people cheerfully.

2. Preparation

Change into your work clothes.

Your personal appearance affects the customer’s impression.

Check your appearance using a mirror or other method.

Make sure your clothes and overall appearance are neat and proper.

3. Daily Inspection

Inspection Before Starting Driving

Receive the truck key and the daily inspection sheet from the operation manager or other responsible person.

Also check whether there are any notices or instructions from the company.

Before driving, carry out the daily inspection of the vehicle.

The daily inspection is done to confirm that the truck can be operated safely.

4. Pre-Operation Roll Call

After completing the daily inspection, receive a face-to-face roll call from the operation manager.

During the roll call, report important matters such as:

Your physical condition

Whether you have alcohol in your body or not

The condition of the vehicle

The result of the daily inspection

Any other necessary information

Listen carefully to the operation manager’s instructions.

If you receive instructions about safe operation, repeat the important points and remember them clearly.

5. Departure

After receiving instructions and confirming that there is no problem with the vehicle, depart according to the company’s instructions.

Drive safely and follow the planned route.

6. Duties During Operation

During operation, carry out your work safely and carefully.

This may include:

Driving to the loading place

Loading cargo

Checking the cargo

Securing the cargo

Driving to the delivery place

Unloading cargo

Reporting if there is any problem

Taking breaks when necessary

Following the operation manager’s instructions

Always follow traffic rules and company rules.

Mid-Operation Roll Call

For long-distance operation of 3 days and 2 nights or longer, contact the business office during the trip.

You must receive a roll call from:

The operation manager, or

An assistant who supports the operation manager

This is called a mid-operation roll call.

7. Post-Operation Roll Call

When the operation is finished and you return to the business office, prepare the operation record.

Give the operation record to the operation manager.

Report and confirm necessary matters.

The operation manager will:

Check your report

Give cautions if necessary

Give guidance if necessary

Give instructions for the next day’s operation

8. Cleaning Up

If there is any problem with the truck you used, report it to the maintenance manager.

If there is no problem:

Clean the truck

Take care of the vehicle

Return the truck key

Keeping the vehicle clean is also part of the driver’s work.

9. Going Home

Complete the procedures for finishing work.

After that, go home.

2. Things Drivers Must Follow

2.1 Responsibility as a Truck Driver

Truck drivers are professionals in transportation.

It is important for truck drivers to have:

Excellent driving skills

A high level of awareness about traffic safety

On the road, trucks drive together with:

Pedestrians

Bicycles

Other vehicles

As professionals, truck drivers must drive while protecting the safety of pedestrians, bicycles, and other vehicles.

Please understand that truck drivers have this responsibility.

2.2 Items That Drivers Must Follow

Truck drivers must follow the following 13 items.

1. Do Not Drive After Drinking Alcohol

Do not drive while under the influence of alcohol.

This includes:

When you have drunk alcohol

When alcohol from the previous day still remains in your body

2. Do Not Drive an Overloaded Truck

Do not drive a truck that is overloaded.

Overloading means loading too much cargo.

3. Load Cargo Correctly

Load cargo using the correct method.

Incorrect loading can cause danger during driving.

4. Take Immediate Action If the Truck Stops Inside a Railway Crossing

If the truck cannot move inside a railway crossing, take the necessary action immediately.

Do not delay.

5. Inform the Company If You Cannot Drive Safely

If you cannot drive safely, inform the company.

This includes cases caused by:

Alcohol influence

Illness

Fatigue

Lack of sleep

Other health or physical problems

6. Carry Out and Confirm Daily Inspection

Carry out the daily inspection of the truck.

After inspection, confirm the condition properly.

7. Receive Roll Calls and Make Required Reports

You must receive the required roll calls, such as:

Pre-operation roll call

Mid-operation roll call

Post-operation roll call

During these roll calls, make the required reports.

8. Stop Driving If You Find a Breakdown or Danger

If you discover a breakdown while driving, or if there is a risk that an accident may happen, stop driving immediately.

Then report the situation to the company.

9. When Changing Drivers, Explain the Situation to the Next Driver

When you finish driving and another driver takes over, tell the next driver about:

The condition of the vehicle

Road conditions

Operation situation

Any problems or important points

10. When Taking Over From Another Driver, Receive a Report and Inspect the Vehicle

When you take over driving from another driver:

Receive a report from that driver

Check the brakes

Check the steering wheel

Check other important parts of the vehicle

11. Prepare the Operation Record

Prepare the work or operation record.

If you use a recording sheet from the operation recorder, write the necessary information on that recording sheet.

12. Carry the Operation Instruction Sheet

Carry the operation instruction sheet prepared by the company.

If the company gives instructions to change the operation plan, write the changed details on the instruction sheet.

13. Do Not Change Gears While Passing Through a Railway Crossing

When passing through a railway crossing, do not operate the transmission or gear.

Keep the same gear and pass through safely.

3. Method and Contents of Daily Inspection

Large trucks can have a major impact on society if a breakdown causes a traffic accident or other problem.

For this reason, daily inspection is required.

By carrying out the daily inspection properly, breakdowns can be prevented.

Points to Remember for Safe and Smooth Daily Inspection

Follow these points when doing daily inspection:

Carry out the inspection on a flat place.

Place wheel chocks on the tires.

Apply the parking brake securely and put the gear in neutral.

Stop the engine and always remove the starter key.

Do not inspect immediately after driving, because there is a risk of burns. Inspect after the engine and other parts have cooled down.

When lifting the cab, follow the correct operating procedure.

Do not drop any objects into the intake duct.

When stepping onto the engine, do not place your foot on pipes, the air cleaner, or similar parts.

After the inspection, check that no cloth, tools, or other items have been left inside the engine room.

Finally, look over the whole vehicle and check for oil leaks, liquid leaks, or water leaks.

4. Receiving Roll Call

4.1 Attitude When Receiving Pre-Operation Roll Call

After completing the daily inspection, the maintenance manager decides whether the vehicle may be used.

After that, you receive a face-to-face roll call from the operation manager.

The pre-operation roll call is done to confirm:

Whether the driver can operate safely

Whether the vehicle can operate safely

Important points for safe operation

Do not receive the roll call only as a formality. Receive it as something useful for safe driving.

If you are staying at a distant place, you must receive the roll call by telephone or another communication method.

When receiving roll call, pay attention to the following points:

Keep your appearance neat and proper.

Understand the purpose of the roll call.

Repeat important instructions and cautions to confirm them.

If there is something you do not understand, ask and confirm it on the spot.

4.2 Important Points for Smooth Pre-Operation Roll Call

To receive the pre-operation roll call smoothly, prepare yourself properly.

Pay attention to the following:

Prepare your appearance and attitude before the roll call.

Do not be late for the roll call time.

Organize the items that must be checked mutually for safe operation.

If you receive instructions about safe operation, repeat the important points and remember them clearly.

4.3 Main Items to Report During Pre-Operation Roll Call

During the pre-operation roll call, mainly report the following items:

Driver’s name

Whether the driver has alcohol in the body

Condition of illness, fatigue, or lack of sleep

Registration number of the vehicle to be driven, or another symbol that identifies the vehicle

Condition of the daily inspection

Other necessary matters

The following points are especially important:

Confirming alcohol condition with an alcohol detector

Reporting whether there is any possibility that you cannot drive safely because of illness, tiredness, lack of sleep, or any other reason

Carrying out or confirming the daily inspection

5. What Is Operation Management?

Operation management means managing drivers and vehicles so that transportation work can be carried out safely, smoothly, and properly.

The operation manager gives necessary instructions and guidance to drivers.

Drivers must follow the instructions of the operation manager.

Operation management includes checking and managing matters such as:

Driver’s physical condition

Alcohol check

Vehicle condition

Daily inspection

Driving route

Driving time

Break time

Rest and sleep

Loading condition

Weather and road conditions

Safe operation instructions

Operation management is not only office work. It is an important system to prevent accidents and protect drivers, companies, customers, and society.

Truck drivers should understand the role of operation management and cooperate with the operation manager.

6. Duties During Operation

6.1 What to Do If You Cause a Traffic Accident

Even if you always try to drive safely, there may be times when you cause an accident.

When an accident happens, take the following actions so that the damage does not become bigger.

1. Rescue Injured People

If you cause a traffic accident, even a small accident, stop driving immediately.

Check the following:

Is anyone injured?

Is any cargo damaged?

Is there any danger around the vehicle?

If there are injured people, ask nearby people for help and rescue the injured people.

Call 119 and ask for an ambulance.

Until the ambulance arrives, give first aid to the injured person.

If the injured person can move to a safe place by themselves, help prevent another accident from happening.

2. Prevent Secondary Accidents

A following vehicle may hit the vehicle involved in the accident. This can make the accident bigger.

To prevent this, move the accident vehicle to a safe place, such as:

The road shoulder

An open area

Another safe place

Also take the following actions:

Turn on the hazard lights.

Use a flare to warn following vehicles.

On an expressway, place a warning triangle or stopping display equipment where following drivers can easily see it.

Place the flare and stopping display equipment behind the vehicle, but only within a range where safety can be maintained.

3. Escape to a Safe Place

After completing the necessary actions, do not remain inside the vehicle or near the road.

Move to a safe place, such as outside the guardrail.

This is especially important on expressways because vehicles may be traveling at 100 km/h.

You must always move to a safe place.

4. Inform the Police

Report the following information to the police:

Date, time, and place of the accident

Number of dead or injured people

Level of injury of the injured people

Damaged items and the level of damage

Cargo loaded on the vehicle

Actions taken at the accident scene

5. Inform the Company

Inform the company about:

The accident

The condition of the cargo

Any other necessary details

Then receive instructions from the operation manager or other responsible person.

If you cannot contact the company yourself because of injury, give the company’s contact information to someone nearby and ask them to contact the company.

6.2 Other Points Drivers Must Be Careful About

1. Actions to Prevent Fire

At an accident scene, oil may be leaking.

Therefore, you must never use fire, such as smoking cigarettes.

If you are carrying dangerous goods, check whether they are leaking or scattered.

If dangerous goods catch fire, the damage can become very large.

Use a fire extinguisher or take other action to put out the fire.

2. Do Not Make a Settlement at the Accident Scene

In a minor accident, the other person may ask you to settle the matter at the accident scene.

A settlement means solving the issue by discussion, including payment for damage, medical treatment, vehicle repair, and other costs.

However, compensation for damage is handled by the company.

The driver must not discuss or agree to a settlement at the accident scene without permission.

Even if the other person strongly asks for a settlement, you must clearly refuse.

3. Confirm Witnesses and Record Information

If the vehicle has a drive recorder, the accident video will be recorded and can be used as evidence.

If there is no drive recorder and there are witnesses, record their information, such as:

Name

Address

Contact information

It is also useful to record or take photos with a smartphone of:

Road conditions at the accident scene

Point of collision

Stopping position of vehicles

Condition of the other party before and after the accident

How the vehicles were damaged

This information helps with accident processing later.

6.3 What to Do If the Vehicle Breaks Down

1. Stop the Vehicle in a Safe Place

If the vehicle breaks down while driving, it is important to stay calm and take proper action to protect your safety and the safety of surrounding vehicles.

On ordinary roads, turn on the hazard lights to inform following vehicles that your vehicle has broken down.

Then reduce speed.

If the vehicle does not slow down even when you press the brake, repeatedly shift down and use engine braking and exhaust braking to reduce speed.

2. Do Not Stop the Engine Until the Vehicle Has Stopped

If the vehicle does not stop even when you press the brake, do not turn off the engine switch.

If you turn off the engine, all electrical power will stop, which is dangerous.

Do not stop the engine until the vehicle has completely stopped.

Before the vehicle becomes unable to move, stop it in a safe place that does not disturb traffic, such as the road shoulder.

Then apply the parking brake.

6.3 What to Do If the Vehicle Breaks Down

Continued

3. What to Do If a Breakdown Happens on an Expressway

If the vehicle breaks down on an expressway, turn on the hazard lights and continue driving carefully.

Try to stop at a:

Service area

Parking area

If you cannot drive that far, do not brake suddenly.

Slow down gradually and stop at:

The road shoulder

An emergency parking area

After stopping, inform following vehicles about the breakdown by using:

A flare

Stopping display equipment / warning triangle

Then report the situation using:

An emergency telephone

Road Emergency Dial: #9910

Emergency telephones are placed every 1 kilometer on expressways. Inside tunnels, they are placed every 200 meters.

When you pick up the emergency telephone, it connects to the road management center.

Report:

The details of the breakdown

The place where you stopped

The kilometer post number on the road shoulder

After the report, traffic management staff may be sent, and information may be shown on road information boards.

These actions help prevent secondary accidents.

4. Report to the Company Immediately If You Notice a Vehicle Problem

If the vehicle cannot move because of a breakdown, report it to the company and receive instructions.

If the vehicle stops in a railway crossing or tunnel, it may lead to a serious accident.

Therefore, if you notice any vehicle problem, report it to the company immediately.

Then receive instructions such as:

Avoid roads with railway crossings or tunnels.

Go to a nearby repair workshop.

Stop operation if necessary.

Do not make your own judgment.

5. Inspect the Broken Part Only in a Safe Place

It is very dangerous to move around the vehicle on an expressway.

If you need to inspect the broken part, do it in a safe place such as a service area.

Even on ordinary roads, avoid inspecting the vehicle in places with heavy traffic.

7. What to Do If Operation Is Delayed

7.1 Respond According to the Cause of the Delay

Arrival time may be delayed for many reasons.

If you do not respond properly, the customer may lose trust in the company.

Possible reasons for delay include:

Late departure

Traffic accident

Vehicle breakdown

Abnormal weather

Traffic restrictions

Traffic congestion

Poor physical condition

How to respond when arrival is delayed should be decided in advance with the operation manager.

Do not contact the customer by your own judgment.

Also, do not try to recover the delay by dangerous driving, such as increasing speed.

7.2 Report to the Company and Receive Instructions

If there is a possibility that arrival time will be delayed, report to the company and receive instructions from the operation manager.

To receive proper instructions, report the following information:

Your name, vehicle number, and business office you belong to

Reason for the delay

Road conditions

Estimated arrival time

Whether there is any accident or breakdown

Especially when the delay is caused by vehicle breakdown, it is very important to report to the company and receive instructions.

8. Handling Emergency Signal Tools and Fire Extinguishers

8.1 Emergency Signal Tools

Emergency signal tools are tools used to inform other drivers about a vehicle breakdown or emergency.

There are two types:

Red flashlight

Flare

A vehicle must carry one of these.

Emergency signal tools should not only be kept inside the vehicle. They must also be checked so that they can be used immediately when needed.

A flare has an expiry period of 4 years.

If a flare is past its expiry date, it may not ignite. Replace it with a new one.

Drivers should also practice how to use it correctly.

How to Use a Flare

Twist the main body and remove the outer cap.

Rub the outer cap, which has match-like chemical material, against the main body.

When rubbing, point the main body forward.

Light the flare.

8.2 Fire Extinguisher

The expiry period of a fire extinguisher is 10 years.

A fire extinguisher that is more than 10 years old must be replaced.

Drivers should confirm where the fire extinguisher is kept in the vehicle.

Correct Way to Use a Fire Extinguisher

Pull the safety pin upward.

Remove the hose and hold the end of the hose toward the source of the fire.

Grip the lever strongly and discharge the extinguisher.

When holding the hose, hold the end of the hose.

Be careful: If you hold the middle part of the hose, the discharge pressure may make it difficult to aim correctly.

9. Measures During Abnormal Weather

9.1 What Is Abnormal Weather?

Abnormal weather means weather conditions that are different from normal and may create danger during driving.

Examples include:

Heavy rain

Strong wind

Snow

Thick fog

Earthquake

Other dangerous weather or natural disasters

During abnormal weather, road conditions can change suddenly.

Visibility may become poor, roads may become slippery, and traffic may become dangerous.

Truck drivers must collect correct information and act calmly.

Important Points During Abnormal Weather

When abnormal weather occurs, do not make careless decisions by yourself.

Report to the company and receive instructions from the operation manager.

Check information from:

Weather reports

Road information boards

Radio

Company instructions

Police or road management information

If driving becomes dangerous, stop at a safe place.

Do not continue driving by force.

9.2 Response to Heavy Rain

During heavy rain, visibility becomes poor and the road surface becomes slippery.

Also, water may collect on the road.

When driving in heavy rain:

Reduce speed.

Keep enough following distance.

Avoid sudden braking.

Avoid sudden steering.

Avoid sudden acceleration.

Use headlights so others can see your vehicle.

Be careful of pedestrians and bicycles.

Watch for flooded roads.

If water is deep, do not enter it carelessly.

A truck may stop because water can enter the engine or electrical parts.

If the rain becomes very strong and driving is dangerous, stop at a safe place and report to the company.

9.3 Response When an Earthquake Warning or Emergency Earthquake Alert Is Issued

When there is a possibility of a large earthquake in a certain area, the government may issue a warning declaration.

When an earthquake warning or emergency earthquake alert is issued, drivers in the affected area must act carefully.

If a warning is issued while driving:

Prepare for the earthquake.

Reduce speed.

Listen to earthquake and traffic information through the car radio or other source.

Act according to the information.

Do not panic.

Do not brake suddenly unless necessary.

If you need to get out of the vehicle and escape, move the vehicle as much as possible to a place outside the road before leaving it.

9.4 When a Major Earthquake Occurs

If a major earthquake happens while you are driving, take the following actions.

1. Stop Safely on the Left Side of the Road

Avoid sudden steering and sudden braking.

Stop the vehicle on the left side of the road in the safest way possible.

2. Collect Information After Stopping

After stopping, listen to earthquake information and traffic information through:

Car radio

Road information

Company instructions

Other reliable sources

Act according to the information you receive.

3. Be Careful If You Continue Driving

If you continue driving after the earthquake, pay close attention to:

Damaged roads

Cracks or holes in the road

Broken traffic signals

Fallen objects

Traffic congestion

Other dangerous road conditions

Do not drive carelessly.

4. If You Leave the Vehicle and Escape

If you must get out of the vehicle and escape, move the vehicle outside the road as much as possible.

If you have to leave the vehicle on the road:

Park it on the left side of the road.

Stop the engine.

Leave the engine key in the vehicle.

Close the windows.

Do not lock the doors.

This allows emergency workers to move the vehicle if necessary.

5. Do Not Block Evacuation or Emergency Vehicles

When parking, do not park in a place that blocks:

People escaping

Ambulances

Fire engines

Police vehicles

Other emergency vehicles

10. Driving According to Truck Characteristics

Trucks have special characteristics that are different from ordinary passenger cars.

Drivers must understand these characteristics and drive safely according to them.

10.1 Vehicle Height and Driving

1. Following Distance May Look Longer Than It Really Is

Following distance means the distance between your vehicle and the vehicle in front.

In a truck, the driver’s seat is high.

Because you look down at the road from a high position, the road surface is easy to see.

Because of this, even if the following distance is short, it may look longer than it really is.

As a result, the driver may unintentionally drive too close to the vehicle in front.

Therefore, always try to keep a longer following distance.

2. Be Careful About Height

Trucks are taller than ordinary vehicles.

Because of this, drivers must be careful about places where the upper part of the truck may hit something.

Be especially careful around:

Overpasses

Bridges

Tunnels

Building eaves

Signboards

Tree branches

Roofs at loading areas

Parking entrances

Always know the height of the truck you are driving.

3. Be Careful When Cargo Makes the Truck Higher

For trucks that are not box-shaped, the total height may become higher when cargo is loaded.

Before driving, check:

The height of the vehicle

The height of the loaded cargo

Whether the route has height restrictions

Do not enter a road or place if the height limit is lower than your truck.

4. Be Very Careful Under Elevated Roads

An elevated-road underpass is a short tunnel-like road under a railway or road.

These places have fixed height limits.

Before entering, always check the permitted height.

If your truck is higher than the allowed height, you must never enter.

To prevent this mistake, always know the exact height of the truck you are driving.

Also, when cargo is loaded, the vehicle may become higher than usual, so be careful.

10.1 Vehicle Height and Driving

Continued

5. Trucks Can Roll Over Easily

Because trucks are tall, they may roll over when:

Driving through curves

Turning right at an intersection

Turning left at an intersection

When driving through curves or turning right or left:

Reduce speed.

Operate the steering wheel carefully.

Operate the brakes carefully.

Do not make sudden movements.

Trucks may also roll over because of strong wind.

Be especially careful on bridges, because the wind can become stronger there.

10.2 Vehicle Length and Driving

Trucks are longer than ordinary passenger cars.

Because of this, drivers must understand the special movement of long vehicles.

1. Be Careful of the Inner-Wheel Difference

When a truck turns right or left, the rear wheels pass more inside than the front wheels.

This difference is called the inner-wheel difference.

The longer the vehicle is, the bigger this inner-wheel difference becomes.

Because of this, when turning left, the rear wheels may hit or run over:

Pedestrians

Bicycles

Motorcycles

Roadside structures

Curbs

Therefore, when turning left, check the left side carefully with mirrors.

Drive slowly and make sure there is no person or object near the side of the truck.

2. Do Not Swing Too Far to the Right Before Turning Left

Some drivers move the truck to the right side before turning left.

This is dangerous.

If you swing too far to the right before turning left:

A motorcycle or bicycle may enter the space on your left.

A following vehicle may misunderstand your movement.

You may disturb vehicles in the next lane.

You may cause an accident.

When turning left, do not move unnecessarily to the right.

Check safety carefully and turn slowly.

3. Rear Overhang Is Large

Rear overhang means the distance between the back end of the vehicle and the center of the rear wheel axle.

Trucks have a large rear overhang.

Because of this, when turning right or left, the rear part of the truck may swing out and hit:

A following vehicle

An oncoming vehicle

A roadside object

When turning right, check the left-side mirror.

When turning left, check the right-side mirror.

Always confirm that the rear part of the truck will not hit anything.

4. Be Careful When Reversing

Because trucks are long, it is difficult to see what is behind the vehicle.

When reversing:

Do not rely only on mirrors.

Check behind the vehicle before reversing.

Reverse slowly.

Stop immediately if you feel danger.

Ask another person to guide you when necessary.

Reversing accidents can easily happen at loading areas, parking lots, and narrow places.

Always check safety carefully before moving backward.

10.3 Vehicle Width and Driving

Trucks are wider than ordinary passenger cars.

Because of this, drivers must be careful on narrow roads and curves.

1. When There Is an Oncoming Vehicle on a Narrow Road, Let the Oncoming Vehicle Go First

When driving on a narrow road and an oncoming vehicle approaches, follow these steps:

Check the left-side mirror and the safety window.

Confirm that there are no pedestrians or bicycles on the left side.

After confirming safety, move carefully toward the left side.

Be careful of signboards and objects above the road.

Stop temporarily.

Let the oncoming vehicle go first.

Before starting again, check that there is no vehicle behind you.

Do not try to force your truck through a narrow place.

2. Be Careful of Bicycles When Starting

When you stop on the left side to give way to an oncoming vehicle, you may block the path of bicycles.

After the oncoming vehicle passes, a bicycle may come out on the right side of your truck.

When starting again, be careful not to miss bicycles coming from behind.

Always check carefully before moving.

3. Be Careful Not to Enter the Opposite Lane on Curves

On a narrow curve with one lane on each side, part of the truck body may enter the opposite lane.

Use the right-side mirror to check the center line carefully.

Drive so that your truck does not cross into the opposite lane.

10.4 Blind Spots and Driving

1. There Are Blind Spots on the Left Side and Left-Rear Side

A blind spot means an area that cannot be seen from the driver’s seat.

Trucks have blind spots on the left side and left-rear side that cannot be seen in the mirrors.

Bicycles traveling on the sidewalk are especially easy to enter these blind spots.

Because of this, drivers may fail to notice them.

When turning left:

Stop temporarily before the crosswalk.

Look to the left and right of the crosswalk.

Confirm that there are no bicycles or pedestrians.

Then turn safely.

2. Do Not Block the Safety Window

The safety window is made to reduce the blind spot on the left side.

If you block this window, you may fail to see pedestrians or bicycles on the left side.

Do not place items near the safety window, such as:

Newspapers

Cardboard boxes

Bags

Other objects

Also, covering the safety window with a curtain or similar item is prohibited.

3. When Reversing, Get Out of the Vehicle and Check Safety

For box-type trucks, the rear side becomes a blind spot.

When reversing, first get out of the truck and check the area behind the vehicle with your own eyes.

If there is a person who can guide you from outside the vehicle, ask that person to guide you.

Even if the truck has a rear-view camera, do not rely only on the camera.

Always confirm safety with your own eyes.

11. Speed and Driving

11.1 Effects of Speed on Driving

Speed has a major effect on truck driving.

The faster you drive, the greater the danger becomes.

1. Impact Force Becomes Larger

The force created when a vehicle hits something is called impact force.

Impact force increases in proportion to the square of speed.

For example, if speed becomes 3 times faster, the impact force becomes:

3 × 3 = 9 times greater

Because trucks are heavy, the impact force becomes even larger.

Driving too fast can lead to a very serious accident.

2. Centrifugal Force Becomes Larger

When you turn the steering wheel sharply on a curve, a force pulls the vehicle outward.

This force is called centrifugal force.

Like impact force, centrifugal force also increases in proportion to the square of speed.

The heavier the vehicle is, the stronger this force becomes.

If cargo is loaded high, there is a danger that the truck may roll over on a curve.

3. Braking Distance Becomes Longer

The distance a vehicle travels from the moment the driver notices danger until the brakes begin to work is called reaction distance.

The distance the vehicle travels from when the brakes begin to work until the vehicle stops is called braking distance.

Together, these two distances are called stopping distance.

Braking distance becomes longer roughly in proportion to the square of speed.

Therefore, the faster you drive, the longer the stopping distance becomes.

4. Distance Traveled in One Second Becomes Longer

The faster you drive, the farther the vehicle moves in just one second.

For example:

Because of this, even a short moment of looking away can cause an accident.

Examples include:

Looking at delivery slips

Looking at a smartphone

Looking away from the road for only a short time

11.2 Controlling Speed

1. Speed According to Weather and Road Conditions

It is important to drive at a suitable speed and keep enough following distance according to conditions such as:

Rain

Snow

Strong wind

Traffic congestion

Road construction

Other road conditions

Do not think only about the legal speed limit. Choose a safe speed according to the actual situation.
