2. Points to Be Careful About on Curves

If you drive too fast on a curve, the vehicle may:

Enter the opposite lane

Go off the road

Roll over

Therefore, reduce speed before entering a curve.

Do not wait until you are already inside the curve to slow down.

3. Points to Be Careful About on Downhill and Uphill Roads

On downhill roads, speed increases easily.

On a long downhill road, if you use the foot brake too much, the brakes may stop working properly.

Therefore, use:

Engine brake

Exhaust brake

Low gear

Do not depend only on the foot brake.

On uphill roads, speed can easily become slow.

Check the speedometer and control your speed.

On expressways and other roads where there is a climbing lane, use the climbing lane.

A climbing lane is a lane for vehicles that become slow on uphill roads.

If vehicles behind you are coming close, it is also important to give way.

12. Mid-Operation Roll Call

Intermediate Roll Call During Duties

When the operation continues for a long distance or for several days, the driver may need to receive a roll call during the operation.

This is called a mid-operation roll call or intermediate roll call.

The purpose is to confirm that the driver and vehicle can continue operating safely.

During the mid-operation roll call, report important information such as:

Your physical condition

Whether you are tired or sleepy

Whether there is alcohol influence

Vehicle condition

Road condition

Cargo condition

Operation progress

Any delay

Any accident, breakdown, or trouble

If the operation manager gives instructions, listen carefully and follow them.

Do not continue driving if you feel that safe operation is difficult.

Report honestly and receive instructions.

13. Post-Operation Duties

After finishing the operation, the driver still has important duties.

These duties help keep the vehicle safe, prepare for the next operation, and report correct information to the company.

13.1 Cleaning and Washing the Vehicle

After the operation is finished, clean the truck.

If necessary, wash the vehicle.

Cleaning and washing are not only for appearance.

They are also important because they help the driver notice problems such as:

Damage

Oil leaks

Water leaks

Loose parts

Tire problems

Dirt or objects that may cause trouble

A clean vehicle gives a good impression to customers and other people.

It also shows the driver’s professional attitude.

13.2 Post-Operation Roll Call

After returning to the business office and finishing the operation, receive a post-operation roll call from the operation manager.

Before the roll call:

Organize the information you need to report.

Write necessary items in the operation record or operation recorder sheet.

Prepare reports about vehicle condition, road condition, and operation condition.

Report the required matters to the operation manager.

Receive instructions about the next operation or your next attendance.

Items to Report During Post-Operation Roll Call

Report or explain the following:

Condition of the vehicle, road, and operation

Matters passed on to the relief driver or replacement driver

Alcohol check result

Other necessary matters

Be honest and accurate when reporting.

If there was any problem, do not hide it.

13.3 Operation Record

After finishing driving work, the driver must prepare the operation record using the required form.

If an operation recorder sheet is used, write the necessary information on that sheet.

The operation record is important because it keeps official information about:

Driving work

Vehicle operation

Time

Route

Breaks

Any problems during the operation

Write the record correctly and do not leave out important details.

Chapter 3

Chapter 3

Cargo Handling Duties

1. Why Is It Necessary to Load Cargo Correctly?

Cargo handling is an important part of a truck driver’s work.

If cargo is not loaded correctly, it can cause many problems, such as:

Cargo collapse

Cargo falling from the truck

Damage to the cargo

Vehicle imbalance

Difficulty in braking or turning

Traffic accidents

Injury to workers or other people

To transport goods safely, securely, and reliably, drivers must understand the characteristics of the cargo and load it correctly.

Correct loading is not only for protecting the cargo. It is also necessary for protecting the driver, other road users, customers, and the company.

2. Shape and Size of Cargo

2.1 Types and Shapes of Cargo

◆ Various Shapes and Sizes of Cargo

Cargo transported by trucks includes many different kinds of goods.

Some cargo is large, such as construction machinery. Some cargo cannot be carried as it is, such as liquids and gases.

Cargo also differs in:

Size

Weight

Shape

Quantity

Packaging type

Some cargo is light. Some cargo is large and heavy.

When loading cargo onto a truck, it is important to think about a loading method that matches the cargo’s shape, size, and quantity.

It is also important to:

Fix the cargo so it does not move

Secure the cargo so it does not fall

Tie the cargo firmly with ropes or other tools when necessary

This is called securing cargo.

◆ Common Packaging Materials

Cargo is often packed or placed in containers to protect it.

For example, items such as rice, vegetables, and fruits are difficult to handle one by one.

Therefore, they are placed in:

Bags

Boxes

Containers

This makes them easier to handle and transport.

2.2 Bagged Cargo

Bagged cargo means goods packed in bags.

Examples include:

Rice

Flour

Fertilizer

Cement

Animal feed

Other powder or grain products

Bagged cargo can easily lose its shape.

When loading bagged cargo:

Place the bags neatly.

Do not leave large gaps.

Do not load them in a way that makes them slide.

Be careful not to tear the bags.

Do not place heavy cargo on weak bags.

Secure the cargo so it does not collapse during driving.

2.3 Cardboard Boxes

Cardboard boxes are commonly used for many types of cargo.

They are easy to stack, but they can also be damaged easily.

When handling cardboard boxes:

Do not crush the boxes.

Do not throw them.

Do not make them wet.

Check the direction marks on the boxes.

Do not stack too many boxes.

Put heavier boxes at the bottom.

Put lighter boxes at the top.

Make sure the boxes do not slide or collapse.

If cardboard boxes are stacked incorrectly, the lower boxes may become crushed and the cargo may be damaged.

2.4 Cans

Canned cargo includes goods packed in metal cans or similar containers.

Examples include:

Paint cans

Oil cans

Food cans

Chemical cans

Other liquid or solid products in cans

When handling cans:

Be careful not to dent or break them.

Do not drop them.

Do not load them in a way that causes rolling.

Check whether there is any leakage.

Secure them firmly so they do not move during driving.

If cans leak, the cargo, vehicle, road, or other goods may be damaged.

2.5 Bulk Cargo

◆ What Is Bulk Cargo?

Bulk cargo means cargo that is not packed individually or grouped together in packaging.

Examples include:

Sand

Petroleum

Wood

Powder cargo

Grain cargo

Drinks and petroleum liquids

Steel frames

Lumber

Utility poles

Bulk cargo is often transported by special trucks such as tank trucks or dump trucks.

However, cargo such as steel frames, lumber, and utility poles may also be transported by ordinary trucks.

◆ Be Careful of Overloading and Cargo Falling

Unlike cargo packed in bags or cardboard boxes, bulk cargo is often difficult to measure by appearance.

Because of this, drivers may accidentally load too much cargo.

If there is a vehicle scale, the weight can be checked.

If there is no scale, the driver must understand the approximate weight and make sure the truck is not overloaded.

Also, take measures so that cargo does not scatter or fall while driving.

For example:

Cover the cargo with a sheet.

Secure the cargo properly.

Check that the cargo will not collapse.

Confirm the cargo condition during operation.

For safe driving, bulk cargo must be loaded so that it does not collapse during the trip.

2.6 Handling Cargo According to Handling Instruction Marks

◆ There Are 19 Types Under JIS

Bags and cardboard boxes sometimes show letters or symbols that explain how the cargo should be handled.

These are called cargo handling instruction marks.

At workplaces, they are sometimes also called care marks.

For cargo in Japan, 19 types of cargo handling instruction marks are decided by JIS.

For exported or imported cargo, the marks are decided by ISO.

Customers may also create their own instruction marks. Therefore, drivers need to understand these marks as well.

Cargo handling instruction marks are used not only to protect the cargo. They are also used to protect the safety of drivers and other people who handle the cargo.

For this reason, drivers must always follow the instructions shown by these marks.

◆ Barcodes Are Also Important

Cargo may also have a barcode in addition to handling instruction marks.

Barcodes are important because they are used to identify and manage cargo.

Be careful not to damage, dirty, or cover barcodes.

If the barcode cannot be read, it may cause problems such as:

Incorrect delivery

Delay in cargo handling

Mistakes in sorting

Difficulty confirming cargo information

Handle cargo carefully so that barcodes remain readable.

3. Types and Use of Pallets

3.1 Role of Pallets

A pallet is a platform used to load cargo together as one unit.

By placing cargo on a pallet, it becomes easier to move the cargo using equipment such as a forklift or hand pallet truck.

Pallets help improve work efficiency and reduce the burden on workers.

They are used for:

Loading cargo

Unloading cargo

Moving cargo inside warehouses

Storing cargo

Transporting cargo safely

When using pallets, drivers must check the condition of the pallet.

Do not use a damaged pallet because it may cause cargo collapse or injury.

Check for problems such as:

Cracks

Breakage

Loose parts

Deformation

Nails or sharp parts sticking out

3.2 How to Stack Cargo on Pallets

Cargo must be stacked on pallets in a stable way.

If the stacking method is wrong, the cargo may collapse during loading, unloading, storage, or driving.

When stacking cargo on a pallet:

Place the cargo neatly.

Do not let cargo stick out too far from the pallet.

Make the height as even as possible.

Put heavy cargo at the bottom.

Put light cargo at the top.

Do not leave large gaps.

Make sure the cargo is balanced.

Use stretch film, bands, or other materials when necessary.

If you do not know how to stack the cargo, especially with a new customer, ask the person in charge at the logistics site and receive instructions.

Basic Pallet Stacking Patterns

There are several basic patterns for stacking cargo on pallets.

The main patterns are:

Block stacking

Alternate row stacking

Brick stacking / interlock stacking

Pinwheel stacking

Understanding these basic patterns is useful for safe and stable loading.

◆ Sudden Driving Operations Are Strictly Prohibited

Even if cargo is stacked correctly on a pallet, it may collapse because of vibration and impact during driving.

The vibration and impact that cargo receives can be bigger than expected, especially when:

Driving over road bumps

Driving on uneven roads

Starting the vehicle

Slowing down

Accelerating

Turning around curves

Therefore, drivers must avoid sudden driving operations such as:

Sudden starting

Sudden braking

Sudden acceleration

Sudden steering

Drive smoothly to protect the cargo and prevent cargo collapse.

4. Loading Cargo onto Trucks and Preventing Cargo Collapse

4.1 Why Does Cargo Collapse Happen?

Cargo collapse means that loaded cargo loses balance, moves, falls, or breaks down from its stacked position.

Cargo collapse may happen because of:

Sudden starting

Sudden braking

Sudden acceleration

Sudden steering

Driving on curves

Driving on rough roads

Vibration during driving

Incorrect loading method

Gaps between cargo

Cargo not being secured properly

Cargo stacked too high

Heavy cargo placed on light cargo

Cargo collapse is dangerous because it can:

Damage the cargo

Injure workers

Cause the truck to lose balance

Cause cargo to fall onto the road

Lead to traffic accidents

Create trouble for the customer and company

Therefore, the driver must load cargo correctly and drive smoothly.

4.2 How to Prevent Cargo Collapse

To prevent cargo collapse, follow these basic points:

Load cargo according to its shape, size, weight, and strength.

Do not leave unnecessary gaps between cargo.

Place heavy cargo at the bottom.

Place light cargo at the top.

Keep the cargo height as low as possible.

Make the left and right weight balance even.

Use sheets, boards, belts, ropes, or other securing materials when necessary.

Check the cargo condition before departure.

Avoid sudden starting, sudden braking, sudden acceleration, and sudden steering.

Check the cargo condition again during operation when necessary.

Cargo that looks stable before departure may still move because of vibration or impact during driving.

Always think about what may happen while the truck is moving.

4.3 Loading Methods That Make Cargo Less Likely to Collapse

◆ Load Cargo Without Gaps

When there are large gaps between cargo, the cargo can move during driving.

If cargo moves, it may collapse.

Place cargo as closely and neatly as possible.

If gaps remain, fill them with proper materials so the cargo cannot move.

◆ Make the Cargo Level and Stable

When the top surface of the cargo is uneven, the cargo becomes unstable.

Try to make the loaded cargo level and balanced.

Do not load cargo in a way that creates a high and unstable pile.

◆ Use Anti-Slip Sheets or Boards

If you place anti-slip sheets or boards between layers of cargo, resistance against sideways slipping increases.

This makes cargo less likely to collapse.

Using sheets or boards can also spread the weight of the cargo.

This helps reduce crushing or deformation of cardboard boxes.

◆ Be Careful with Cardboard Box Strength

Cardboard boxes become weaker depending on how they are stacked.

According to the text:

Because the strength of boxes changes depending on the stacking method, choose a method that keeps the cargo stable and does not crush the lower boxes.

Practical Points for Safe Loading

Before finishing loading, check:

☐ Is the cargo balanced from left to right? ☐ Is the cargo balanced from front to back? ☐ Are heavy items placed at the bottom? ☐ Are fragile items protected? ☐ Are there no large gaps? ☐ Is the cargo secured so it cannot move? ☐ Are ropes, belts, sheets, or boards used correctly? ☐ Is the cargo height safe? ☐ Can doors, gates, or side panels close safely? ☐ Is there any danger that cargo may fall when the door is opened?

4.4 Materials and Equipment for Loading Cargo So That It Does Not Collapse

◆ Understand the Features and Correct Use of Materials and Equipment

To prevent cargo collapse, it is important to understand the characteristics of the cargo and load it correctly.

However, correct loading alone cannot completely prevent cargo collapse caused by vibration and impact during driving.

At work sites, many types of materials and equipment are used to prevent cargo accidents.

The names of these materials and equipment may differ depending on:

The customer

The company

The region

Therefore, it is important to collect and understand this information at each work site.

Materials and Equipment Used to Fill Gaps

These are used to prevent gaps between cargo.

Examples include:

Airbags

Cardboard

Styrofoam

Pallets

If gaps remain between cargo, the cargo may move during driving.

Fill the gaps properly so that the cargo does not move.

Materials and Equipment Used to Fix Cargo

These are used to fix cargo so that it does not move.

Examples include:

Lashing belts

Wire ropes

Synthetic fiber ropes

Steel belts

Use the correct fixing tool according to the type, weight, and shape of the cargo.

Materials and Equipment Used to Prevent Cargo from Hitting Each Other

These are used so that cargo items do not hit each other.

Examples include:

Cardboard

Plywood

Pallets

Plastic boards

This helps prevent damage to cargo during transport.

Materials and Equipment Used to Reduce Contact Between Cargo and Fixing Tools

These are used to prevent direct contact or reduce impact between the cargo and the fixing tools.

Examples include:

Corner protectors

Protective cloth

They help prevent cargo from being damaged by belts, ropes, or other securing tools.

Materials and Equipment Used to Prevent Cargo Collapse on Pallets

These are used to prevent cargo on pallets from collapsing.

Examples include:

Stretch film

Anti-vibration materials for pallets

Anti-slip sheets

Horizontal bands

These materials help keep palletized cargo stable during loading, unloading, and driving.

Care and Maintenance of Materials and Equipment

Some materials and equipment are thrown away after use.

Others can be used repeatedly.

For reusable materials and equipment, it is important to clean, maintain, and store them properly after use.

They must always be kept in a condition where they can be used safely at any time.

Check Deterioration Through Daily Inspection

The most effective way to check deterioration is daily inspection.

Deterioration means that the performance or quality of the material or equipment becomes worse.

This inspection is important for:

Getting the customer’s trust

Checking the condition of materials and equipment

Using them safely

Be especially careful with materials and equipment used to fix cargo.

If fixing tools are damaged or weak, the cargo may move or collapse during driving.

4.5 Methods of Securing Cargo So That It Does Not Collapse

Securing cargo means fixing cargo firmly so it does not move, fall, or collapse during driving.

The correct securing method depends on:

Type of cargo

Weight of cargo

Shape of cargo

Size of cargo

Vehicle type

Road conditions

Driving distance

Basic Points for Securing Cargo

When securing cargo, remember these points:

Use securing tools suitable for the cargo.

Do not use damaged belts, ropes, or wires.

Tighten the cargo firmly, but do not damage it.

Use corner protectors when belts or ropes touch sharp edges.

Make sure the cargo cannot move forward, backward, left, or right.

Check the securing condition before departure.

Check again during operation if necessary.

After driving for some time, cargo may settle or shift, so tighten again if needed.

Be Careful with Round Cargo

When loading round cargo, it may roll easily.

Use wheel stoppers or chocks so it cannot roll.

The height of the stopper should be at least one-tenth of the cargo’s diameter.

Be Careful When Loading Cargo of Different Shapes and Sizes

When loading cargo with different shapes and sizes, gaps may appear in the front, back, left, or right.

These gaps happen because the cargo has different:

Weight

Size

Shape

Use wood or other materials to fill the gaps.

Make sure the cargo cannot move.

Final Check Before Departure

Before departure, check:

☐ Are all gaps filled? ☐ Are belts, ropes, and wires tightened correctly? ☐ Are corner protectors used where needed? ☐ Is pallet cargo wrapped or banded properly? ☐ Is round cargo stopped from rolling? ☐ Is cargo protected from hitting other cargo? ☐ Are reusable tools in good condition? ☐ Is there no damaged or weak securing equipment? ☐ Will the cargo stay stable during sudden braking or curves?

5. Danger of Uneven Load

Uneven load means a condition where the weight of the cargo is applied only to one part of the truck bed.

5.1 Causes and Dangers of Uneven Load

◆ Unbalanced Loading Has a Bad Effect on Driving

Unbalanced loading can lead to truck rollover.

To drive stably, it is important that the cargo’s center of gravity is in the center of the truck.

Uneven loading means that the center of gravity of the cargo is shifted away from the center of the truck, either:

To the left or right

To the front or rear

If the center of gravity shifts to the left or right, it affects steering operation.

In some cases, the truck may roll over.

Some drivers may have experienced situations such as:

The truck turns left or right even when the steering wheel is not turned.

The truck does not go straight unless the steering wheel is held firmly.

If this happens while driving, the road shape may be one reason, but you should also suspect that the center of gravity has shifted to the left or right.

Effect on Braking Distance

If the center of gravity shifts forward or backward, it can affect the distance needed to stop after applying the brakes.

If the center of gravity is toward the front of the truck, the truck may take longer than usual to stop, even if the brakes are applied in the same way.

Also, in large trucks, if heavy cargo is loaded too much toward the front or rear of the truck bed, a large load may be placed on one axle.

This may exceed the axle load limit of 10 tons, so drivers must be careful.

Uneven Loading Is Caused by the Loading Method

Uneven loading that greatly affects truck driving is mainly caused by how the cargo is loaded.

If cargo is loaded in an unbalanced way, it may move or collapse during driving.

As a result, the position of the center of gravity may change.

When different kinds of cargo are loaded together, special care is needed.

This is especially true when the cargo is:

Heavy

Irregular in shape

Machinery or similar items

Difficult to judge by appearance

For square boxes such as cardboard boxes, it is easier to understand the center of gravity.

However, when cargo has different shapes, the center of gravity is difficult to understand.

If many such items are loaded together, judging the center of gravity becomes even more difficult.

5.2 How to Load Cargo So That Uneven Load Does Not Occur

◆ Do Not Decide by Yourself

If the shape of the cargo is complicated and the center of gravity is difficult to understand, you must consult:

The operation manager

The customer

The person in charge at the work site

Receive proper instructions and guidance.

Do not make decisions only by yourself.

You must make every effort to load the cargo safely.

Experience is important and can help reduce mistakes.

However, depending too much on experience can also lead to a serious mistake.

Do not think, “I have done this before, so it will be fine.”

Always confirm safety.

Practical Points to Prevent Uneven Load

When loading cargo, check the following:

☐ Is the cargo balanced from left to right? ☐ Is the cargo balanced from front to back? ☐ Is heavy cargo placed in a safe position? ☐ Is the center of gravity too high? ☐ Is cargo secured so it will not move during driving? ☐ Are gaps filled properly? ☐ Have you received instructions if the cargo shape is complicated? ☐ Is the truck within the allowed axle load and loading capacity?

5.3 Do Not Drive an Overloaded Truck

◆ Overloading Has a Bad Effect on Driving

Drivers must not drive a business truck that is overloaded.

Overloading is not only a traffic violation.

It can also cause many dangerous effects, such as:

Longer braking distance

Poor vehicle balance

Dirty air caused by exhaust gas

Damage to the vehicle

Damage to roads

Because of this, overloading must never be allowed.

Measures Taken Against Overloaded Vehicles

If a police officer judges that a vehicle is overloaded, the vehicle may be stopped.

At that time, the driver must show the vehicle inspection certificate.

The weight of the loaded cargo may be measured.

If overloading is confirmed, the driver may be ordered to:

Remove the cargo that exceeds the weight limit

Transfer some cargo to another vehicle

Take other necessary measures

If these measures cannot be taken, the driver may receive a traffic instruction document.

This document gives instructions about measures to prevent danger, such as traffic lane or route instructions.

Customers Must Not Request Overloading

Customers are also prohibited from asking drivers to overload a truck.

They are also prohibited from handing over cargo when they know that it will cause overloading.

If a customer repeatedly asks for overloading, the customer may receive orders or warnings from the authorities.

If a customer asks you to overload the truck, contact your company immediately.

5.4 How to Use Operation Recorders and Drive Recorders, and Their Effects

◆ Use and Effects of Operation Recorders

By using an operation recorder, you can check important driving information such as:

Whether the truck exceeded the maximum speed

Whether the driver drove at a steady speed

Whether the driver suddenly increased or decreased speed

By checking both the operation recorder and the work record, the company can also confirm:

Whether the driving time exceeds an average of 9 hours per day over 2 days

Whether the driver drove continuously for more than 4 hours

Whether the driver took proper breaks

If the truck exceeds the fixed speed on an expressway, there may be a problem with the speed limiter.

A speed limiter is a device that controls the truck’s maximum speed to 90 km/h.

The operation recorder can also be used to check whether the vehicle has such a problem.

◆ Use and Effects of Drive Recorders

A drive recorder records video while the vehicle is moving.

It can help check:

Driving behavior

Dangerous situations

Sudden braking

Accidents

Near-miss incidents

By reviewing drive recorder footage, drivers and companies can understand what happened and improve safety guidance.

It is useful not only after an accident, but also for preventing future accidents.

6. Overloading

6.1 Causes of Accidents Due to Overloading and Their Social Impact

1. Causes of Accidents Due to Overloading

◆ Bad Effects Caused by Overloading

Every truck has a fixed amount of cargo weight that it can carry.

Overloading means loading cargo beyond the permitted weight and driving the truck in that condition.

When a truck is overloaded, it becomes more unstable than when it carries the correct amount of cargo.

Because of this, accidents become more likely to happen.

Overloading is extremely dangerous.

◆ Impact Force Becomes Greater

If a heavy object and a light object are dropped from the same height, the heavy object causes more damage when it hits the ground.

Also, even the same object causes more damage if it is dropped from a higher place.

This is because the force at the time of impact depends on:

The weight of the object

The speed of the object

This force is called impact force.

Impact force increases in proportion to the weight of the object and in proportion to the square of speed.

For example:

A collision with a hard wall at 60 km/h has about the same impact force as falling from a height of 14 meters.

A collision at 120 km/h has about the same impact force as falling from a height of 56 meters.

When a truck is overloaded, the vehicle becomes heavier.

Therefore, if it crashes, the impact force becomes much greater.

◆ Braking Distance Becomes Longer

When a truck is overloaded, the vehicle becomes heavy.

Because of this, it becomes harder to stop.

Even if the driver applies the brakes in the same way, the stopping distance becomes longer than usual.

This increases the risk of rear-end collisions and serious accidents.

◆ Vehicle Balance Becomes Worse

Overloading makes the truck unstable.

The vehicle may become difficult to control, especially when:

Turning curves

Changing lanes

Going downhill

Braking suddenly

Driving in strong wind

A heavy and unstable truck can easily lose balance.

This can lead to rollover accidents or cargo collapse.

◆ Tires and Vehicle Parts Are Damaged More Easily

When a truck carries more weight than allowed, a heavy burden is placed on:

Tires

Brakes

Suspension

Axles

Engine

Other vehicle parts

This may cause breakdowns or tire bursts.

If this happens while driving, it can lead to a serious accident.

6.2 Effects on Roads and Other Facilities

◆ Overloading Causes Serious Damage to Roads

Overloading also has bad effects on roads, bridges, and other facilities.

Roads and bridges are built based on vehicle weights decided by law.

If a vehicle exceeds certain weight limits, permission is required before it can pass.

Examples of these limits include:

Gross vehicle weight: 20 tons Some roads allow up to 25 tons.

Axle load: 10 tons Some tractors are exceptions.

Wheel load: 5 tons

When overloaded vehicles pass, they can create dents in roads and damage bridges.

This not only damages public facilities, but also disturbs the safe driving of other vehicles.

◆ Road Damage Becomes Extremely Large

According to an experiment, when one 20-ton vehicle passes on a road, it causes about the same amount of road damage as approximately 4,000 vehicles of 10 tons.

Even though overloaded large vehicles are only about 0.3%, they cause more than 90% of the damage to roads and bridges.

This shows that overloading is not only a problem for one driver or one company.

It is a serious problem for society as a whole.

6.3 What Truck Drivers Must Do to Prevent Overloading

To prevent overloading, truck drivers must follow these points:

Know the maximum loading capacity of the truck.

Do not load more than the permitted weight.

Check the weight of the cargo when possible.

Do not judge only by appearance.

Be careful with bulk cargo because its weight is hard to understand by looking.

If the cargo seems too heavy, confirm with the company or the person in charge.

If a customer asks for overloading, report to the company immediately.

Never drive an overloaded truck.

Follow the instructions of the operation manager.

Remember that overloading can cause accidents, road damage, and social problems.

Important Reminder

Overloading is dangerous because it affects:

The driver

Other road users

The cargo

The truck

The company

Roads and bridges

Society as a whole

A professional truck driver must never think, “It is only a little over the limit.”

Even a small overload can create a big danger.

7. Prevention of Work Accidents

Cargo-handling work includes many dangerous tasks.

Drivers may be injured when:

Loading cargo

Unloading cargo

Climbing onto the truck bed

Getting down from the truck bed

Securing cargo with ropes

Removing ropes

Putting on or removing sheets

Using roll box pallets

Using forklifts

A professional truck driver must prevent traffic accidents and also prevent work accidents.

7.1 Points to Be Careful About During Loading and Unloading Work

◆ Use Safe Climbing Equipment

When getting on or off the truck bed, use proper climbing equipment.

Climbing equipment includes:

Steps installed on the truck

Movable steps or platforms prepared at the work site

Tailgate lifter used as a step when stopped at a middle position

Do not use something unsafe just because it looks convenient.

For example, a side guard or underrun-prevention guard is not climbing equipment. It is not safe for getting on or off the truck.

When working at a place higher than 1.5 meters, climbing equipment is required in principle.

◆ Prevent Falls From the Truck Bed

Falls from the truck bed can cause serious injuries.

Be especially careful when:

The truck bed is wet

The truck bed is slippery

You are carrying cargo

You are tired

You are working in a hurry

The work area is dark

The cargo is unstable

Do not jump down from the truck bed.

Always use steps or proper equipment.

◆ Check the Work Area Before Starting

Before loading or unloading, check the surrounding area.

Confirm:

☐ Is the ground flat? ☐ Is there enough space for work? ☐ Are there pedestrians or other workers nearby? ☐ Are there forklifts or other vehicles moving nearby? ☐ Is the cargo stable? ☐ Is the truck stopped safely? ☐ Is the parking brake applied? ☐ Are wheel chocks used when necessary?

◆ Do Not Work Under Unstable Cargo

Never place your body under cargo that may fall.

Cargo may collapse suddenly because of:

Vibration

Wrong loading

Gaps between cargo

Weak packaging

Uneven load

Sudden movement of the truck

Before starting work, check whether the cargo may fall or collapse.

7.2 Rope-Tying and Rope-Removing Work

◆ Rope-Tying Work

When tying cargo with ropes, follow these points:

Inspect the rope before using it.

Use a dry rope.

When two people work together, check with each other while working.

When tightening the rope, face your body toward the vehicle. Open your feet diagonally and pull the rope vertically.

When working on top of cargo, keep your posture low.

At the front of the truck bed, face toward the driver’s seat.

At the rear of the truck bed, turn your back toward the driver’s seat.

Place protective material on the corners of cargo where the rope touches.

Attach the rope so that it connects the hooks on both sides in a straight line.

◆ Rope-Removing Work

When removing ropes, follow these points:

Work on a flat place.

Stop the engine.

Apply the parking brake.

Use wheel chocks.

Before removing the rope, check that there is no danger of cargo or protective materials falling.

Do not stand on top of the cargo while removing the rope.

7.3 Sheet-Covering and Sheet-Removing Work

◆ Sheet-Covering Work

When covering cargo with a sheet, work carefully because the sheet may be heavy and difficult to handle.

Pay attention to the following points:

Do not work alone if the sheet is heavy.

Work with two people when necessary.

Be careful not to lose your balance.

Do not stand in an unstable place.

Watch your feet carefully.

Be careful of wind, because the sheet may suddenly move.

Secure the sheet properly so it will not come loose while driving.

◆ Sheet-Removing Work

When removing the sheet, follow these points:

Check that there is no abnormal condition with the cargo.

Remove the rubber cords.

Pull the sheet down with two people.

Fold the sheet so that it will be easy to spread again later.

When placing the sheet on the roof carrier of the cab, first put the sheet on the truck bed, and then place it on the roof carrier.

If the sheet is heavy, always work with two people.

Practical Safety Reminder

During cargo-handling work, do not rush.

Many injuries happen because workers think:

“This will only take a moment.”

“I can do it alone.”

“I have done this many times.”

“It is probably safe.”

A professional driver must always check safety first.

7.4 Loading and Unloading Work for Van Body Trucks

A van body truck is a truck with a closed box-shaped cargo compartment.

When loading or unloading cargo from a van body truck, workers may fall from the cargo bed or be injured by cargo movement.

Therefore, always work carefully.

Important Safety Points

Stop the truck on a flat and safe place.

Apply the parking brake.

Use wheel chocks when necessary.

Open the rear door carefully.

Before opening the door fully, check whether cargo is leaning against the door.

Do not stand directly behind the door if cargo may fall.

Check the condition of the cargo before unloading.

Do not pull cargo out carelessly.

Use proper equipment when handling heavy cargo.

Work with another person when the cargo is heavy or unstable.

Be Careful When Opening the Rear Door

Cargo may have moved during driving.

When you open the rear door, cargo may suddenly fall out.

Before opening the door:

Check whether the truck is level.

Open the door slowly.

Keep your body away from the door opening.

Watch for cargo that may fall.

Do not stand in the path of falling cargo.

7.5 Handling Roll Box Pallets

A roll box pallet is a wheeled cage or box used to move cargo.

It is useful, but it can also be dangerous if handled incorrectly.

Common dangers include:

The roll box pallet moving suddenly

Fingers or feet getting caught

The pallet falling over

Cargo falling from the pallet

The pallet rolling down a slope

Workers being hit by the pallet

Basic Points for Handling Roll Box Pallets

When handling roll box pallets, follow these points:

Check the wheels and stoppers before use.

Do not use a damaged roll box pallet.

Do not overload it.

Do not stack cargo too high.

Make sure the cargo does not collapse.

Push slowly and carefully.

Do not run while pushing it.

Be careful on slopes and uneven floors.

Use the stopper when stopping.

Keep your hands and feet away from places where they may get caught.

Working With a Tailgate Lifter

When using a tailgate lifter, follow these basic rules:

Do not exceed the maximum load of the lifter.

Do not go under the lifter.

Work on a level surface.

When raising or lowering the lifter:

Operate the tailgate lifter while standing away from the lifter.

Workers must not ride on the lifter while operating it.

When the lifter is at ground level:

Be very careful of the step or height difference between the lifter and the ground.

When moving a roll box pallet from the lifter to the ground, first push it slightly toward the truck bed.

Release the stopper with your foot.

Pull it out slowly and carefully.

8. Handling Forklifts

Forklifts are very useful for cargo-handling work, but they can cause serious accidents if used incorrectly.

Only qualified and authorized people should operate forklifts.

Drivers must understand forklift danger even if they do not operate the forklift themselves.

Important Points During Forklift Cargo Handling

Follow these points during forklift cargo-handling work:

Take measures so that cargo does not collapse.

Do not load cargo beyond the allowed load limit.

Be careful about the fork spacing so that the load is not unbalanced.

Do not hit the cargo with the tips of the forks.

Never allow anyone under the forks or under lifted cargo.

Never allow anyone to ride on the forks or on a pallet.

Do not take cargo directly from forks that are raised.

Do not let your body stick out from the driver’s seat.

Important Points When Parking or Stopping a Forklift

When parking or stopping a forklift:

Stop the engine.

Apply the parking brake.

Lower the forks completely.

Use wheel chocks.

Park in the designated place.

Remove the key.

Chapter 4

Chapter 4

Hazard Prediction Training

Hazard Prediction Training means training to predict different dangers in driving situations and think about how to drive in order to avoid those dangers.

It is also called KYT.

KYT Sheet 1

Turning Left at an Intersection with Traffic Lights

You are going to turn left at an intersection with traffic lights.

Think about:

What dangers are there in this situation?

How should you drive to avoid those dangers?

Possible Dangers

1. Involvement Accident with a Motorcycle

A motorcycle may be coming toward the intersection from behind on the left side.

If you turn left without checking carefully, you may巻き込み — cut in and hit or drag the motorcycle into the turn.

2. Collision with a Bicycle

A bicycle may be coming toward the intersection on your left side.

When you turn left, you may hit the bicycle at the crosswalk.

3. Hitting a Pedestrian

An oncoming vehicle waiting to turn right may be stopped before the crosswalk.

A pedestrian may come out from the far side of that stopped vehicle and start crossing.

If you do not check carefully, you may hit the pedestrian.

Safe Driving Method

Before turning left, always check whether motorcycles or bicycles are approaching from the left side or from behind.

Be especially careful not to miss bicycles coming from the left side of the crosswalk.

Stop before the crosswalk and check the left and right sides of the crosswalk.

Turn left slowly only after confirming safety.

KYT Sheet 2

Approaching an Intersection When the Signal Turns Yellow

You are approaching an intersection where the traffic light has changed to yellow.

On the sidewalk across the intersection, there are children.

A vehicle coming from the opposite lane is giving a right-turn signal.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. Rear-End Collision

If you try to pass through the intersection when the signal turns yellow, the vehicle in front may stop.

In that case, you may hit the vehicle from behind.

2. Collision with an Oncoming Right-Turning Vehicle

If the vehicle in front passes through the intersection and you follow it, you may enter the intersection without enough checking.

You may then collide with the oncoming vehicle that is turning right.

3. Hitting Children Crossing the Road

If you follow the vehicle in front and enter the intersection, the signal may change.

Children may then begin crossing the road, and you may hit them.

Safe Driving Method

Do not assume that the vehicle in front will pass through the intersection just because the signal has turned yellow.

The meaning of a yellow signal is to stop before the intersection, except when you are already close to the stopping position and cannot stop safely.

When the signal turns yellow, slow down early.

Stop before the intersection.

Even if the vehicle in front enters the intersection, do not simply follow it.

KYT Sheet 3

Approaching an Intersection with Poor Visibility

You are approaching an intersection in a residential area where visibility is poor.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. Collision from the Left Side

When you try to pass through the intersection, a pedestrian, bicycle, or vehicle may come from the left side of the crossing road.

They may be hidden in the blind spot created by buildings, so you may not see them until late.

2. Collision from the Right Side

When you try to pass through the intersection, a pedestrian, bicycle, or vehicle may come from the right side of the crossing road.

They may be hidden by an oncoming vehicle or by buildings.

This can lead to a collision.

Safe Driving Method

Even if there is no stop sign or stop marking, you are required to slow down at intersections with poor visibility.

Always slow down and check whether pedestrians, bicycles, or vehicles are approaching from the left or right side of the crossing road.

When there is an oncoming vehicle, it becomes easier to miss pedestrians, bicycles, or vehicles coming from the right side of the crossing road.

Check carefully and proceed only after confirming safety.

KYT Sheet 4

Driving on a Road Without a Center Line

You are driving on a narrow road with no center line.

An oncoming vehicle is approaching from the front.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. Contact with an Oncoming Vehicle

Because the road is narrow, if you continue driving without slowing down, your truck may come too close to the oncoming vehicle.

This can cause side contact or a collision.

2. Hitting Pedestrians or Bicycles on the Left Side

When trying to avoid the oncoming vehicle, you may move too far to the left.

If there are pedestrians or bicycles on the left side, you may hit them.

3. Hitting Roadside Objects

If you move too far left, your truck may hit:

Utility poles

Signs

Walls

Guardrails

Trees

Other roadside objects

Because trucks are wide and tall, you must also check objects above the road.

Safe Driving Method

Do not try to force your truck through a narrow road.

Slow down early when you see an oncoming vehicle.

Check the left side with mirrors and the safety window.

Confirm that there are no pedestrians, bicycles, or obstacles.

Move carefully to the left only after confirming safety.

Stop temporarily if necessary.

Let the oncoming vehicle pass first.

Before starting again, check behind and around the truck.

KYT Sheet 5

Driving on a Downhill Curve

You are driving on a downhill curve.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. Rear-End Collision

On a downhill road, your speed can easily increase.

If the vehicle in front slows down and you do not control your speed early, you may hit it from behind.

2. Collision with an Oncoming Vehicle

On a curve, the vehicle in front may enter the opposite lane and collide with an oncoming vehicle.

Also, your own truck may cross the center line and collide with an oncoming vehicle.

3. Rollover on an S-Curve

If it is an S-shaped curve, poor steering or braking operation can cause the truck to roll over.

If the truck rolls over, an oncoming vehicle may also collide with it.

Safe Driving Method

Reduce speed before entering the curve.

Use engine braking and exhaust braking on the downhill road.

Do not depend only on the foot brake.

Keep enough following distance from the vehicle in front.

Do not make sudden steering movements.

Do not brake suddenly while turning.

Stay within your lane and be careful not to cross the center line.

Drive especially carefully on S-curves.

KYT Sheet 6

Driving on a Road with a Crosswalk Ahead

You are driving on a road where there is a crosswalk ahead.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. Pedestrians May Start Crossing

A pedestrian may suddenly start crossing at the crosswalk.

If you do not slow down early, you may not be able to stop in time.

2. Pedestrians May Be Hidden by Other Vehicles

A pedestrian may be hidden behind a parked or stopped vehicle near the crosswalk.

If you pass without checking carefully, you may hit the pedestrian.

3. Bicycle May Cross Quickly

A bicycle may enter the crosswalk or bicycle crossing from the side.

Because bicycles move faster than pedestrians, they may appear suddenly.

Safe Driving Method

When approaching a crosswalk, check both sides carefully.

If you cannot clearly confirm that nobody is crossing, slow down so you can stop before the crosswalk.

If pedestrians or bicycles are crossing or trying to cross, stop before the crosswalk.

Do not overtake or pass another vehicle near a crosswalk carelessly.

Keep enough distance from the vehicle in front.

Be prepared to stop at any time.

KYT Sheet 7

Turning Right from a Parking Lot and Entering the Road

You are leaving a customer’s parking lot.

You will cross the sidewalk, turn right, and enter the road.

There is a vehicle in the opposite lane that is trying to enter the parking lot.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. Hitting a Pedestrian or Bicycle on the Sidewalk

When crossing the sidewalk from the parking lot, pedestrians or bicycles may be coming from the left or right.

If you only look at the road and do not check the sidewalk carefully, you may hit them.

2. Collision with a Vehicle on the Road

When entering the road by turning right, vehicles may be coming from both directions.

If you enter the road too quickly, you may collide with a vehicle traveling on the road.

3. Contact with the Vehicle Entering the Parking Lot

There is a vehicle in the opposite lane trying to enter the parking lot.

If you move out at the same time, your truck may come too close to that vehicle.

This can cause contact or collision.

4. Hitting a Wall or Gatepost

Because trucks have a large inner-wheel difference, if the entrance or exit is narrow, the truck may hit a wall, gatepost, or other object when turning out of the parking lot.

Safe Driving Method

Before crossing the sidewalk, stop temporarily.

Check carefully for pedestrians and bicycles from both left and right.

Do not block pedestrians or bicycles.

Check vehicles coming from both directions on the road.

Do not rush to enter the road.

Give way to the vehicle trying to enter the parking lot if necessary.

Turn slowly while checking mirrors.

Be careful of the truck’s inner-wheel difference and rear overhang.

Confirm the length and size of your truck before driving.

KYT Sheet 8

Passing Through a Railway Crossing

You are approaching a railway crossing.

There is a bicycle ahead on the left side.

Ahead of the railway crossing, there is an intersection with traffic lights, and the signal is red.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. Getting Stuck Inside the Railway Crossing

Because the traffic signal beyond the railway crossing is red, vehicles ahead may stop.

If you enter the crossing without checking the space beyond it, your truck may stop inside the railway crossing.

This is extremely dangerous.

2. Hitting the Bicycle on the Left Side

A bicycle is traveling on the left front side.

If you move too close to the left side or pass without checking carefully, you may hit the bicycle.

3. Panic When the Alarm Starts

If the railway crossing alarm starts while your truck is still inside or near the crossing, you may panic.

This can lead to sudden braking or unsafe movement.

Safe Driving Method

Before entering the railway crossing, check whether there is enough space beyond it for the whole truck.

If the road ahead is congested or the signal is red, do not enter the crossing.

Stop before the crossing and wait.

Keep a safe distance from the bicycle.

Do not force your truck past the bicycle.

Pass through the crossing only after confirming that you can completely clear it.

Do not change gears while crossing.

Drive slowly and carefully.

KYT Sheet 9

Driving on a Rainy Expressway

You are driving on an expressway in the rain.

The speed is restricted to 60 km/h.

You feel that the vehicle in front is slow, so you are thinking about changing lanes into the overtaking lane.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. Skidding on a Wet Road

In rainy weather, the road surface is wet and slippery.

If you change lanes suddenly, the truck may skid.

2. Missing a Vehicle Behind You

In rain, it is harder to see vehicles behind you in the mirrors.

If you change lanes without clearly confirming the rear, you may collide with a following vehicle in the overtaking lane.

3. Rear-End Collision

If the vehicle in front slows down further and you are too close, you may hit it from behind.

Rain increases stopping distance, so this danger becomes greater.

4. Aquaplaning / Hydroplaning

At higher speeds on wet roads, tires may lose contact with the road surface because of water.

This can make steering and braking ineffective.

Safe Driving Method

When a speed restriction is shown, always obey the restricted speed.

In rainy weather, avoid unnecessary lane changes.

Keep enough following distance.

Do not make sudden steering movements.

Do not brake suddenly.

Use mirrors carefully, but remember that visibility in mirrors is poor during rain.

Continue driving calmly in your lane unless changing lanes is truly necessary.

KYT Sheet 10

Driving on a Congested Expressway

You are driving on an expressway.

There is traffic congestion ahead, and vehicles are stopped.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. Rear-End Collision with Stopped Vehicles

On an expressway, your speed is high.

If you notice the traffic congestion too late, you may not be able to stop in time and may hit the last vehicle in the traffic jam.

This can become a very serious accident.

2. Sudden Braking by the Vehicle in Front

The vehicle in front may suddenly brake after noticing the congestion.

If your following distance is too short, you may collide with it from behind.

3. Collision During Lane Change

Some vehicles may suddenly change lanes to avoid the traffic jam.

If you also change lanes without checking carefully, you may collide with another vehicle.

Safe Driving Method

Look far ahead and check traffic conditions early.

Pay attention to brake lights of vehicles ahead.

Keep enough following distance.

Reduce speed early when you notice congestion.

Avoid sudden braking.

Turn on hazard lights when necessary to warn following vehicles.

Do not change lanes suddenly.

Stop safely behind the traffic jam with enough distance.

KYT Sheet 11

Driving on a Snowy Road

You are driving on a road where snow is falling or snow has accumulated.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. Skidding Because the Road Is Slippery

When snow is falling or snow has accumulated, the road surface becomes slippery.

The truck may skid easily when:

Braking

Turning

Starting

Changing lanes

2. Stopping Distance Becomes Longer

On snowy roads, the stopping distance becomes much longer than usual.

If you drive too close to the vehicle in front, you may not be able to stop in time.

3. Road Markings Become Difficult to See

When snow covers the road, road markings become hard to see.

Important information for driving may be missed.

For example, road markings showing a crosswalk or bicycle crossing may be hidden by snow. Because of this, you may fail to notice a pedestrian with an umbrella who is trying to cross.

Safe Driving Method

Reduce speed.

Keep a longer following distance than usual.

Avoid sudden braking.

Avoid sudden steering.

Avoid sudden acceleration.

Be careful because road markings may be hidden by snow.

Watch carefully for pedestrians and bicycles.

Drive with the understanding that stopping distance is longer on snowy roads.

KYT Sheet 12

Driving on a Road with a Bicycle Lane

You are driving on a one-lane road with a sidewalk and a bicycle lane.

A bicycle is traveling in the bicycle lane.

Ahead of that bicycle, there is a parked vehicle.

In the mirror, you can see a delivery bicycle coming from behind.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. The Bicycle Ahead May Move Into Your Lane

The bicycle in the bicycle lane may try to avoid the parked vehicle ahead.

It may suddenly move to the right and enter your driving lane.

If you continue driving without slowing down, you may hit the bicycle.

2. The Delivery Bicycle Behind May Come Close

A delivery bicycle is approaching from behind.

If you slow down, turn, or move left without checking carefully, the bicycle may come too close to your truck.

3. The Parked Vehicle May Create a Blind Spot

The parked vehicle may hide pedestrians, bicycles, or other road users.

A person may suddenly appear from around the parked vehicle.

Safe Driving Method

Do not overtake the bicycle ahead carelessly.

Predict that the bicycle may move right to avoid the parked vehicle.

Reduce speed early.

Keep enough side distance from the bicycle.

Check the mirror for the delivery bicycle behind.

Do not force your truck between the bicycle and the parked vehicle.

Let the bicycle go first when necessary.

Be careful of pedestrians or bicycles hidden by the parked vehicle.

KYT Sheet 13

Starting from a Freight Vehicle Roadside Parking Area

You are going to start moving from a roadside parking area reserved for freight vehicles.

There is a guardrail between the road and the sidewalk.

There is a parked vehicle ahead, and another vehicle is approaching from behind.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. Collision with the Vehicle Approaching from Behind

When starting from the parking area, a vehicle may be approaching from behind.

If you start without checking carefully, you may collide with that vehicle.

2. Contact with the Parked Vehicle Ahead

There is a parked vehicle in front.

If you start and move out without enough space, the front or side of your truck may hit the parked vehicle.

3. Difficulty Seeing Pedestrians or Bicycles

Because there is a guardrail between the road and the sidewalk, visibility may be limited.

Pedestrians or bicycles may be difficult to see.

4. The Truck May Block Traffic

If you enter the road slowly or stop halfway, following vehicles may have to brake suddenly.

This can create danger and traffic disturbance.

Safe Driving Method

Before starting, check the rear carefully with mirrors.

Confirm the speed and distance of the vehicle approaching from behind.

Do not start if the approaching vehicle is too close.

Give a signal early.

Check the parked vehicle ahead and make sure there is enough space.

Start slowly and smoothly.

Do not enter the traffic lane suddenly.

Be careful of pedestrians and bicycles near the sidewalk and guardrail.

Join traffic only after confirming it is safe.

KYT Sheet 14

Turning Right at an Intersection with Traffic Lights

You are going to turn right at an intersection with traffic lights.

An oncoming vehicle is approaching.

There may also be pedestrians or bicycles near the crosswalk after you turn.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. Collision with an Oncoming Vehicle

If you turn right while an oncoming vehicle is approaching, you may collide with that vehicle.

Even if you think there is enough time, a truck needs more time to turn than a passenger car.

2. Hitting a Pedestrian on the Crosswalk

When turning right, you may focus only on oncoming vehicles and fail to check the crosswalk.

A pedestrian may be crossing after you turn.

3. Hitting a Bicycle After the Turn

A bicycle may enter the crosswalk or bicycle crossing quickly.

If you do not check beyond the crosswalk, you may hit the bicycle.

Safe Driving Method

Do not turn right while an oncoming vehicle is approaching.

Wait until the oncoming vehicle passes.

Turn right only after confirming safety.

When turning right, check not only the crosswalk but also the area beyond the crosswalk.

Proceed at a speed where you can stop at any time.

KYT Sheet 15

Turning Left at an Intersection with Traffic Lights

You are going to turn left at an intersection with traffic lights.

A motorcycle is approaching from behind on the left side.

A bicycle can be seen beyond the intersection.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. Involvement Accident with a Motorcycle

The motorcycle approaching from the left rear may enter the left side of your truck.

If you turn left without checking, you may巻き込み — catch or drag the motorcycle into the turn.

2. Collision with a Bicycle

The bicycle beyond the intersection may enter the crosswalk or bicycle crossing.

If you only check the immediate left side and do not check ahead, you may hit the bicycle while turning.

3. Hitting a Pedestrian or Bicycle Hidden in a Blind Spot

Because trucks have large blind spots, pedestrians or bicycles near the left side may be difficult to see.

If you turn left without stopping or slowing enough, an accident may happen.

Safe Driving Method

Before turning left, check the left rear carefully.

Use mirrors and direct visual checks as much as possible.

Be especially careful of motorcycles and bicycles entering the left side.

Reduce speed before the turn.

Stop before the crosswalk if necessary.

Check the crosswalk and bicycle crossing carefully.

Turn left slowly only after confirming safety.

KYT Sheet 16

Driving on a Road in a Residential Area

You are driving on a road in a residential area.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. Children May Suddenly Run Out

In residential areas, children may suddenly run out from between houses, parked vehicles, or walls.

If you drive too fast, you may not be able to stop in time.

2. Pedestrians or Bicycles May Appear from Blind Spots

Pedestrians or bicycles may come out from:

Between parked vehicles

Side streets

Building entrances

Behind walls or fences

Because trucks have large blind spots, you must check carefully.

3. Contact with Parked Vehicles or Roadside Objects

Residential roads are often narrow.

Your truck may come close to:

Parked vehicles

Utility poles

Walls

Signs

Trees

Bicycles parked on the roadside

Safe Driving Method

Reduce speed in residential areas.

Be ready to stop at any time.

Look far ahead and also check both sides.

Be careful near parked vehicles and side streets.

Do not drive too close to pedestrians or bicycles.

Keep enough space from roadside objects.

If visibility is poor, slow down more and proceed carefully.

KYT Sheet 17

Driving on an Expressway at Night

You are driving on an expressway at night.

There may be a broken-down vehicle stopped on the shoulder ahead.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. Late Discovery of a Broken-Down Vehicle

At night, visibility is poor.

If you notice a broken-down vehicle stopped on the shoulder too late, you may collide with it.

2. Collision with a Following Vehicle During Lane Change

If you suddenly change lanes to avoid the broken-down vehicle without checking behind, you may collide with a following vehicle.

3. Rear-End Collision

If the vehicle in front suddenly slows down after noticing danger, you may hit it from behind if your following distance is too short.

Safe Driving Method

Use headlights properly.

Look far ahead.

Keep enough following distance.

Be careful of vehicles stopped on the shoulder.

Do not change lanes suddenly.

Before changing lanes, check mirrors carefully.

Confirm the speed and distance of following vehicles.

Reduce speed early if you see danger ahead.

KYT Sheet 18

Driving on a Road with a Bus Stop

You are driving on a road where there is a bus stop.

A bus may be stopped at the bus stop, and pedestrians may be nearby.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. Pedestrians May Cross the Road to Get on the Bus

A pedestrian on the left side of the road may try to cross the road to get on the bus.

If you do not slow down, you may hit the pedestrian.

2. Pedestrians May Come Out from Behind the Bus

A pedestrian hidden behind the bus may suddenly cross the road.

You may not see them until very late.

3. Collision with an Oncoming Vehicle Overtaking the Bus

An oncoming vehicle may try to overtake the bus.

If that vehicle enters your lane, you may collide with it.

Safe Driving Method

Slow down when approaching a bus stop.

Be careful of pedestrians near the bus.

Predict that someone may cross from in front of or behind the bus.

Do not pass the bus carelessly.

Watch for oncoming vehicles that may overtake the bus.

Keep a speed where you can stop immediately.

Proceed only after confirming safety.

KYT Sheet 19

Driving on a Two-Lane Road on One Side

You are driving on a road with two lanes on one side.

Think about:

What dangers are there?

How should you drive safely?

Possible Dangers

1. Collision During Lane Change

Another vehicle may be traveling in the next lane.

If you change lanes without checking carefully, you may collide with it.

2. Missing a Motorcycle or Bicycle

Motorcycles may enter the blind spot beside or behind the truck.

Because trucks have large blind spots, it is easy to miss small vehicles.

3. Rear-End Collision

If traffic ahead slows down and you are too close to the vehicle in front, you may hit it from behind.

4. Contact When Passing Parked or Stopped Vehicles

If there is a stopped vehicle, parked vehicle, or obstacle ahead, you may need to change lanes.

If you move suddenly, you may disturb vehicles in the next lane.

Safe Driving Method

Keep enough following distance.

Look far ahead and predict changes in traffic flow.

Before changing lanes, check mirrors carefully.

Check blind spots before moving.

Give a signal early.

Do not make sudden lane changes.

Confirm the speed and distance of vehicles in the next lane.

Drive smoothly and safely.

This completes Chapter 4: Hazard Prediction Training / KYT Sheets.

フォームの終わり

Chapter 1

Chapter 1

Basics for Truck Drivers

Truck Drivers کے بنیادی اصول

1. Trucks Support People’s Daily Lives and Work

Trucks لوگوں کی روزمرہ زندگی اور کام کو support کرتے ہیں

1.1 The Role of Trucks

Trucks کا کردار

آپ شاید اب Japan میں truck driver بننے کے لیے study شروع کر رہے ہیں۔

سب سے پہلے یہ سمجھنا ضروری ہے کہ Japan میں trucks کا role بہت important ہے۔

Trucks مختلف قسم کی چیزیں transport کرتے ہیں، جیسے:

daily food products روزمرہ کھانے پینے کی چیزیں

clothing and general goods کپڑے اور عام استعمال کی چیزیں

industrial products and parts industrial products اور parts

construction materials تعمیراتی سامان

petroleum and gas جیسے liquids اور gases تیل، gas وغیرہ

huge bridges اور plant equipment جیسے special items بہت بڑے bridge parts یا factory equipment

Trucks home delivery parcels بھی deliver کرتے ہیں، اور convenience stores کے goods بھی پہنچاتے ہیں۔

Transport trains، ships اور airplanes کے ذریعے بھی ہوتا ہے، لیکن جب goods station، port یا airport تک پہنچ جاتے ہیں، تو final destination تک لے جانے کا کام اکثر trucks کرتے ہیں۔

Japan میں domestic freight یعنی ملک کے اندر goods transport کا 90% سے زیادہ حصہ trucks کے ذریعے ہوتا ہے۔

اس لیے truck drivers واقعی essential workers کہلا سکتے ہیں۔

Essential Worker کا مطلب

Essential worker وہ شخص ہوتا ہے جس کا کام society کی daily life کو چلانے کے لیے ضروری ہو۔

آسان Summary

Japan میں trucks صرف goods نہیں اٹھاتے، بلکہ لوگوں کی daily life، shops، factories، hospitals اور businesses کو چلانے میں مدد کرتے ہیں۔

Truck driver کا کام بہت responsible کام ہے، کیونکہ اگر goods timely نہ پہنچیں تو پورا society system متاثر ہو سکتا ہے۔

1.2 What Would Happen If Truck Transport Stopped?

اگر Truck Transport رک جائے تو کیا ہوگا؟

Truck transport لوگوں کے کام اور daily life کے لیے بہت ضروری ہے۔

اگر پورے Japan میں truck transport رک جائے، تو products، parts، medicines اور دوسری ضروری چیزیں deliver نہیں ہو سکیں گی۔

اس کے نتیجے میں:

shops اپنا کام جاری نہیں رکھ سکیں گی

factories کو parts نہیں ملیں گے

hospitals کو medicines اور medical items نہیں ملیں گے

construction sites پر material نہیں پہنچے گا

markets میں goods کی کمی ہو جائے گی

daily life بہت متاثر ہو گی

Great East Japan Earthquake کی مثال

Japan میں 2011 کے Great East Japan Earthquake کے فوراً بعد fuel shortage ہوئی۔

Fuel shortage کی وجہ سے بہت سے trucks نہیں چل سکے۔

کچھ time کے لیے لوگ convenience stores اور دوسری shops سے products آسانی سے نہیں خرید سکے۔

اس سے معلوم ہوتا ہے کہ اگر trucks نہ چلیں تو daily life فوراً متاثر ہو جاتی ہے۔

Spain Strike کی مثال

Europe اور Asia میں بعض اوقات strikes کی وجہ سے trucks رک جاتے ہیں۔

مثال کے طور پر Spain میں tanker trucks strike کی وجہ سے رک گئے۔

اس وجہ سے gasoline gas stations تک نہیں پہنچ سکا، fuel shortage ہو گئی، اور عام لوگوں کو serious problems face کرنا پڑیں۔

لوگوں کے لیے shopping، hospital جانا اور daily movement مشکل ہو گیا۔

Practical Point for Students

Truck drivers کو سمجھنا چاہیے کہ truck transport صرف driving job نہیں ہے۔

یہ Japan کی:

economy

daily life

medical system

factories

markets

construction

shops

سب کو support کرتا ہے۔

اگر trucks goods transport نہ کریں، تو society کے بہت سے systems رک سکتے ہیں۔

آسان Summary

Truck transport بند ہو جائے تو Japan کی daily life، hospitals، shops، factories اور markets سب متاثر ہوں گے۔

Truck driver کا کام society کے لیے بہت important ہے۔

1.3 Driver’s License System and the Difference Between Business Trucks and Private Trucks

Driving License System اور Business Trucks / Private Trucks کا فرق

Driver’s License System

Driving License کا نظام

Japan میں truck چلانے کے لیے truck کے size کے مطابق مناسب driving license چاہیے۔

آپ کون سا vehicle چلا سکتے ہیں، یہ license کی type پر depend کرتا ہے۔

License Types

1. Large Vehicle License

Large Vehicle License / بڑے vehicle کا license

اس license سے آپ یہ vehicles چلا سکتے ہیں:

large vehicles

medium vehicles

semi-medium vehicles

ordinary vehicles

small special vehicles

2. Medium Vehicle License

Medium Vehicle License / درمیانے vehicle کا license

اس license سے آپ یہ vehicles چلا سکتے ہیں:

medium vehicles

semi-medium vehicles

ordinary vehicles

small special vehicles

3. Semi-Medium Vehicle License

Semi-Medium Vehicle License

اس license سے آپ یہ vehicles چلا سکتے ہیں:

semi-medium vehicles

ordinary vehicles

small special vehicles

4. Ordinary Vehicle License

Ordinary Vehicle License / عام گاڑی کا license

اس license سے آپ یہ vehicles چلا سکتے ہیں:

ordinary vehicles

small special vehicles

Important Note

Truck driver بننے کے لیے صرف ordinary car license کافی نہیں ہو سکتا۔

Truck کے size اور weight کے مطابق correct license ضروری ہے۔

Difference Between Business Trucks and Private Trucks

Business Trucks اور Private Trucks کا فرق

Japan میں trucks کی دو main types ہیں:

1. Business Trucks

کاروباری trucks

2. Private Trucks

نجی استعمال کے trucks

Business Trucks

کاروباری Trucks

Business truck وہ truck ہے جو ایک trucking company استعمال کرتی ہے۔

اس company کو Minister of Land, Infrastructure, Transport and Tourism سے permission ملی ہوتی ہے۔

Business trucks customers کا سامان payment کے بدلے transport کر سکتے ہیں۔

License Plate

Business truck کی number plate:

green background

white letters

ہوتی ہے۔

اسے green-number truck بھی کہتے ہیں۔

Private Trucks

Private Trucks / نجی trucks

Private truck وہ truck ہے جو کوئی company، shop یا individual اپنے own goods لے جانے کے لیے استعمال کرتا ہے۔

Private truck customers کا سامان payment کے بدلے transport نہیں کر سکتا۔

License Plate

Private truck کی number plate:

white background

green letters

ہوتی ہے۔

اسے white-number truck بھی کہتے ہیں۔

آسان فرق

Business Truck

Customer کا سامان payment لے کر لے جا سکتا ہے۔ Number plate green background + white letters ہوتی ہے۔

Private Truck

صرف اپنا سامان لے جا سکتا ہے۔ Customer کا سامان payment پر نہیں لے جا سکتا۔ Number plate white background + green letters ہوتی ہے۔

اہم الفاظ

Truck Transport Truck کے ذریعے سامان پہنچانا

Domestic Freight ملک کے اندر goods transport

Essential Worker Society کے لیے ضروری کام کرنے والا worker

Driver’s License Driving license

Large Vehicle License Large vehicle کا license

Medium Vehicle License Medium vehicle کا license

Semi-Medium Vehicle License Semi-medium vehicle کا license

Ordinary Vehicle License عام vehicle کا license

Business Truck Payment لے کر customer goods transport کرنے والا truck

Private Truck اپنے سامان کے لیے استعمال ہونے والا truck

Green-number Truck Business truck

White-number Truck Private truck

آسان Summary for Students

Japan میں truck چلانے کے لیے truck کے size کے مطابق correct license چاہیے۔

Business truck customer کا سامان payment کے بدلے لے جا سکتا ہے، جبکہ private truck صرف اپنا سامان لے جا سکتا ہے۔

Truck driver کو license system اور truck type کا فرق اچھی طرح سمجھنا چاہیے۔

2. Social Impact of Truck Accidents

Truck Accidents کے Social Effects

Truck کا accident صرف ایک simple traffic accident نہیں ہوتا۔ کیونکہ trucks بڑے اور heavy ہوتے ہیں، اس لیے accident serious ہو سکتا ہے اور society پر بڑا اثر ڈال سکتا ہے۔

2.1 Truck Accidents That Have a Major Social Impact

Truck accidents جن کا society پر بڑا اثر ہوتا ہے

Goods کو safely، securely اور reliably transport کرنے کے لیے safety ہمیشہ top priority ہونی چاہیے۔

Traffic accidents صرف لوگوں کی جان یا injuries کا سبب نہیں بنتے، بلکہ company کے management اور reputation پر بھی اثر ڈالتے ہیں۔

Trucks عام vehicles کے مقابلے میں:

size میں بڑے ہوتے ہیں

weight میں heavy ہوتے ہیں

accident میں damage زیادہ ہو سکتا ہے

اگر truck accident ہو جائے، تو یہ آسانی سے serious accident بن سکتا ہے۔

Accident کے Effects

Truck accident کا اثر صرف victim پر نہیں پڑتا، بلکہ:

victim کی family

truck driver

trucking company

trucking industry

society as a whole

سب متاثر ہو سکتے ہیں۔

ایک truck driver کا accident بہت سے دوسرے truck drivers اور trucking companies کی image کو بھی affect کر سکتا ہے۔

Professional Driver کی Responsibility

Business truck driver عام driver سے مختلف ہوتا ہے۔

Business truck driver ایک professional driver ہوتا ہے۔

اس لیے truck driver کو دو important باتیں ہمیشہ ذہن میں رکھنی چاہییں:

1. Professional Awareness

آپ truck driving کو اپنا profession سمجھ کر responsible driving کریں۔

2. Strong Belief

آپ کے دل میں یہ مضبوط یقین ہونا چاہیے:

“مجھے accident ہرگز نہیں کرنا۔”

Practical Point for Students

Truck driver صرف vehicle نہیں چلا رہا ہوتا۔ وہ company، customer، cargo اور society کی safety کی responsibility بھی لے رہا ہوتا ہے۔

اس لیے careless driving، overconfidence، smartphone use، sleepiness یا fatigue کو lightly نہیں لینا چاہیے۔

2.2 Situation of Rear-End Collisions

Rear-End Collisions کی Situation

Rear-end collision کا مطلب ہے: پیچھے سے آگے والی گاڑی کو hit کرنا۔

Business trucks کے traffic accidents میں تقریباً half accidents rear-end collisions ہوتے ہیں۔

Rear-End Collisions کی Common Causes

Rear-end collisions عموماً ان وجوہات سے ہوتے ہیں:

careless driving بے احتیاط driving

enough attention نہ دینا مکمل دھیان نہ ہونا

looking away while driving driving کے دوران نظریں ہٹانا

drowsy driving نیند والی حالت میں driving

overworked driving بہت زیادہ تھکاوٹ کے ساتھ driving

Ordinary Roads پر Rear-End Collisions

Ordinary roads پر اکثر rear-end collision اس وقت ہوتا ہے جب truck low speed پر traffic میں آگے والی گاڑی کو hit کرتا ہے۔

مثلاً:

traffic jam میں

signal کے قریب

slow moving traffic میں

parking یا stopping vehicles کے پیچھے

Expressways پر Rear-End Collisions

Expressways پر rear-end collisions زیادہ serious ہو سکتے ہیں۔

Common situation:

Truck high speed پر چل رہا ہوتا ہے، اور traffic jam کے last vehicle کو hit کر دیتا ہے۔

یہ accident بہت خطرناک ہو سکتا ہے کیونکہ speed زیادہ ہوتی ہے۔

Practical Safety Rule

Rear-end collision سے بچنے کے لیے:

آگے والی گاڑی سے safe distance رکھیں

road ahead کو far distance تک دیکھیں

smartphone نہ دیکھیں

نیند یا fatigue ہو تو driving نہ کریں

sudden braking کے لیے ready رہیں

speed control کریں

2.3 Situation of Intersection Accidents

Intersection Accidents کی Situation

Business trucks کے fatal accidents میں pedestrians اور bicycles کے ساتھ intersections پر ہونے والے accidents تقریباً 30% تک ہوتے ہیں۔

Intersection یعنی road crossing پر truck driver کو خاص احتیاط کرنی چاہیے۔

Common Accident Patterns

1. Left Turn کے وقت Bicycle Accident

جب truck intersection پر left turn لیتا ہے، تو bicycles کے ساتھ accidents عام ہوتے ہیں۔

Japan میں vehicles left side پر چلتے ہیں، اس لیے left turn کے وقت blind spots اور side area خاص طور پر خطرناک ہو سکتے ہیں۔

2. Right Turn کے وقت Pedestrian Accident

جب truck right turn لیتا ہے، تو pedestrians کے ساتھ accidents عام ہوتے ہیں، خاص طور پر crosswalk پر۔

Intersection پر Important Checks

Truck driver کو intersection پر یہ چیزیں ضرور check کرنی چاہییں:

pedestrians

bicycles

motorcycles

oncoming vehicles

traffic signals

crosswalk

blind spots

side mirrors

left/right turning area

آسان Summary

Truck accident society پر بہت بڑا اثر ڈال سکتا ہے۔

Rear-end collisions business trucks کے accidents میں بہت common ہیں، اور intersections پر pedestrians اور bicycles کے ساتھ serious accidents ہو سکتے ہیں۔

Professional truck driver کو ہمیشہ یہ سوچ کر drive کرنا چاہیے:

“Safety first — accident never.”

2.4 Prevention of Accidents Caused by Health Problems

Health Problems کی وجہ سے accidents کی prevention

Health problem کی وجہ سے accident کا مطلب ہے:

Driver کو driving کے دوران illness یا physical problem ہو جائے، اور وہ driving جاری نہ رکھ سکے۔

Truck drivers عموماً long hours drive کرتے ہیں، اس لیے health بہت important ہے۔

اگر driver کی physical condition خراب ہو تو:

attention کم ہو جاتی ہے

concentration کم ہو جاتی ہے

reaction slow ہو جاتا ہے

correct decision لینا مشکل ہو جاتا ہے

یہ سب accident کا سبب بن سکتے ہیں۔

Lifestyle-related Diseases

Lifestyle سے related بیماریاں

اگر lifestyle-related diseases کو ignore کیا جائے تو driving کے دوران serious problem ہو سکتی ہے۔

Examples:

High Blood Pressure

بلند فشار خون

Diabetes

شوگر

Obesity

موٹاپا

یہ conditions اگر control میں نہ ہوں تو driving کے دوران:

stroke

heart attack

serious accident

کا risk بڑھ سکتا ہے۔

Health Problem Accidents کو prevent کرنے کے طریقے

Truck drivers کو چاہیے کہ:

1. Regular health checkups کروائیں

اپنی health condition کو regularly check کریں۔

2. Enough sleep لیں

Sleep کم ہو تو driving خطرناک ہو سکتی ہے۔

3. Moderate exercise کریں

Body کو healthy رکھنے کے لیے مناسب exercise ضروری ہے۔

4. Alcohol زیادہ نہ پئیں

Alcohol health اور driving دونوں کے لیے dangerous ہے۔

5. Physical condition carefully manage کریں

اگر طبیعت خراب ہو، fever ہو، dizziness ہو، chest pain ہو، یا نیند پوری نہ ہو تو company کو inform کریں۔

Practical Note for Students

Truck driver کو یہ نہیں سوچنا چاہیے:

“تھوڑی طبیعت خراب ہے، پھر بھی drive کر لیتا ہوں۔”

Professional driver کو اپنی health condition honestly report کرنی چاہیے، کیونکہ ایک health problem serious accident کا سبب بن سکتی ہے۔

2.5 Use of Safety Management Devices

Safety Management Devices کا استعمال

Traffic accidents prevent کرنے کے لیے مختلف safety management devices استعمال کیے جاتے ہیں۔

دو important devices یہ ہیں:

1. Digital Tachograph

2. Drive Recorder

Digital Tachograph

Digital Tachograph / Driving Data Recorder

Digital tachograph vehicle operation کی information record کرتا ہے۔

یہ information include ہو سکتی ہے:

speed

driving time

operation time

Model کے مطابق یہ بھی record ہو سکتا ہے:

sudden braking

sudden starts

fuel consumption

other driving data

Digital Tachograph کا فائدہ

Company اس record کو analyze کر کے driver کو guidance دے سکتی ہے:

Safe Driving

Safe driving بہتر کرنے کے لیے

Eco-driving

Fuel saving اور environment-friendly driving کے لیے

یہ device driver کے driving behavior کو سمجھنے اور improve کرنے میں مدد دیتا ہے۔

Drive Recorder

Drive Recorder / Video Recorder

Drive recorder vehicle چلتے وقت video record کرتا ہے۔

اگر sudden braking، impact یا collision ہو تو یہ event سے پہلے اور بعد کی video record کر سکتا ہے۔

کچھ models continuous video recording بھی کرتے ہیں۔

Drive Recorder کا فائدہ

Drive recorder سے:

accident situation سمجھنے میں مدد ملتی ہے

driver behavior check کیا جا سکتا ہے

safety guidance دی جا سکتی ہے

accident prevention میں مدد ملتی ہے

evidence کے طور پر use ہو سکتا ہے

آسان Summary

Health management truck driver کے لیے safe driving کی بنیاد ہے۔

Health problems، lack of sleep، fatigue، high blood pressure، diabetes اور obesity کو ignore کرنا dangerous ہے۔

Safety devices جیسے Digital Tachograph اور Drive Recorder driving behavior record کرتے ہیں اور accidents prevent کرنے میں مدد دیتے ہیں۔

اہم الفاظ

Health Problem Accident Health issue کی وجہ سے ہونے والا accident

Physical Condition جسمانی حالت

Attention دھیان

Concentration توجہ

High Blood Pressure بلند فشار خون

Diabetes شوگر

Obesity موٹاپا

Stroke فالج / brain attack

Heart Attack دل کا دورہ

Regular Health Checkup باقاعدہ health check

Digital Tachograph Driving data record کرنے والا device

Drive Recorder Driving video record کرنے والا device

Sudden Braking اچانک brake لگانا

Fuel Consumption Fuel استعمال ہونا

Student Formula

Good Health + Enough Sleep + Safe Devices + Honest Reporting = Safe Truck Driving

3. Attitude Required as a Truck Driver

Truck Driver کے لیے ضروری رویہ / Attitude

Truck driver صرف vehicle نہیں چلاتا، بلکہ company کی image، customer کا trust، cargo کی safety اور road users کی حفاظت کی responsibility بھی لیتا ہے۔

اس لیے truck driver کو professional attitude، polite behavior اور proper manners رکھنے چاہییں۔

3.1 Greetings Inside the Company

Company کے اندر Greetings

Human relationships greetings سے شروع ہوتے ہیں۔

Company کے اندر اچھے relations بنانے کے لیے greetings بہت important ہیں۔

Greetings کے Important Points

Smile کے ساتھ greet کریں۔

Cheerful voice میں greet کریں۔

Greeting کرتے وقت سامنے والے کی آنکھوں کی طرف دیکھیں۔

پہلے خود greeting کریں، دوسرے کا انتظار نہ کریں۔

Practical Examples

おはようございます

Good morning صبح بخیر

お疲れ様です

Good work / Thank you for your work آپ نے محنت کی / کام کے لیے شکریہ

よろしくお願いします

Please guide/support me براہِ کرم تعاون فرمائیں

3.2 Greetings Outside the Company

Company کے باہر Greetings

Customers truck drivers سے یہ توقع رکھتے ہیں کہ وہ:

goods کو carefully handle کریں

time کی پابندی کریں

proper clothes پہنیں

neat appearance رکھیں

polite language استعمال کریں

اچھے manners اور attitude دکھائیں
